# Continuity

Rules of record. Anything below is canon. If a batch needs to change one of these, record the change here in the same commit, with the chapter it changed in.

**THE ARCHIVE, AND HOW TO READ THIS FILE.** The world-rules spine at the top and every Volume 7 section are the
hot file. **The established facts, running ledgers, construction budgets and repairs of Volumes 1 to 6 have been
MOVED to `state/archive/continuity-before-volume-07.md`**, with a stub left under each original heading so a
search still finds them here. **Nothing was deleted.** `state/INDEX.md` maps every section in this run's state
files to where it now lives. **Read the top of this file; reach for the archive only when a fact needs its
precedent, and do not re-derive an archived figure — it is the record of what was measured at the time.**

## World rules

1. Cultivation has seven realms of nine stages: Body-Forging, Condensing, Foundation, Golden Core, Nascent Soul, Severance, Immortal Vessel. The Last Ascension is a thirteenth thing, not a realm.
2. Advancing past stage nine of a realm inside the bound requires a sect **seal** as well as a body. Servants are not given it.
3. Advancing inside the bound is **announced** to everyone in the bound at once. There is no private breakthrough.
4. Announcements are audible, never written. They report and never advise.
5. Announcements **can be edited** before they are broadcast. Three counties' copies of one announcement may differ. Discovered at the Volume 1 midpoint.
6. Death by harvest leaves an **account** that follows a family three generations.
7. Qi is finite. Running dry is the most common cause of death under thirty.
8. Nothing inside the bound repairs an aperture. Damage is permanent and visible.
9. **The world has no named weeks and no named weekdays.** A day is an hour of the day and a day of a month; a month is thirty days, so month-day = `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1`, day 134 being the first of a month. Characters say *four days up and four days down*, *the third of the month*, *the second hour of the afternoon*. **Narration may not name a weekday, in any register and from any mouth.** Written into canon at the Volume 4 close review; all eight standing instances in Volumes 1 and 2 were repaired the same day.

## The layer's rules

1. Yuan can read every cultivator inside the bound and touch nothing. No hands, no body, no qi.
2. He can **designate** one Chosen One at a time, anywhere inside the bound. Designating costs him the memory of his own name.
3. The layer honours a Chosen One's quests. Rewards land. Qi answers her a half-step faster.
4. The layer cannot be lied to about a quest's cost, and neither can Yuan.
5. He cannot grant what he cannot pay for.
6. She can refuse him. A refused designation begins to rot and he cannot stop it.
7. **Settlement** rises every time he uses the channel. It only falls when **Merit** is spent.
8. Merit is spent from **her foundation**. It does not come back. She gets stronger and less solid at once.
9. The tax takes **sequence**, not substance: the technique and the order, not the body's capacity. Practice rebuilds substance and cannot rebuild order.
10. He can act through her hand and body. It costs more than the tax and it is partial.
11. He can read the shape of a technique on a living person and **cannot describe it in words**. He can only find it.
12. He is read by the layer, not reading it. He cannot see who writes it back.
13. The designation holds whether or not she speaks to him and whether or not she wants it (ch 37).
14. Advancements are announced in a fixed four-clause order — where, who, what was let go, what is held — and that order is the order a body must be moved in. Empty first, then take (ch 40, ch 43).
15. Something walks a row in the layer once a day and looks at him. Four or five breaths. Never missed. It asks nothing and takes nothing (ch 43).
16. A cracked aperture is a channel as well as a bill. Qi that leaks sideways into a held pattern does work, and is paid for at the rim on every use (ch 39).

## Registers

- Yuan: every sentence in the **present tense**. No other character is ever in the present tense.
- The layer: square-bracket lines, on their own line, **at most one per chapter**. Never bold, never italics, never a heading.
- Chapter markdown: title heading, optional section headings, plain paragraphs, scarce bold/italic. No blockquotes, no code blocks, no tables, no horizontal rules.

## Prose

- Paragraphs two to four sentences. Break over about 90 words. Never over about 120.
- Chapters land around 1,800–2,700 words unless the scene needs longer. **Floor 1,300.** A batch averaging under 1,300, or whose word counts fall monotonically across the batch, is a failed batch: add scene material inside the existing cards, never pad. Never record a length trade-off.
- **Never put outline language inside a character's thought or narration** — *this chapter, this volume, the next chapter, in this batch, the whole series, the reader*. Say what the character knows. This leaked four times in batch 0008 and twice more in closed Volume 1; all are now gone.
- Long accumulating sentences are the voice, but a revelation gets short plain sentences at the point of impact.
- One simple sentence about one person after any stretch of machinery.
- Never: "his eyes widened", "he couldn't believe it", "suddenly", "little did he know".
- Construction watch: *nobody asked, nobody was thanked, there is no form, there is no column, the record remained, nobody knew.* Effective once or twice each, then dead. Used in batch 0001: furnace column (ch 1,12). Do not reuse without new meaning.

## Content

- No explicit sexual content. Intimacy is earned and goes off page.
- Ning Yan is seventeen in Volume 1 and the book does not romanticise that. Nothing in Volumes 1 and 2 is a romance.
- All sexual characters are adults.
- No intimacy involving Yuan. He has no body and that is not a problem to be solved.
- Real people do not appear as characters.

## Named and reserved

Yuan (was Yuan Sheng), Ning Yan, Ning Xue, Ning Bo, Ning Wu, Qiu Zhulang, Bai Zhu, Shuyin, Du Hong, Mei Jiu, Shen Xiu, Feng Lushi, Wei Ran, Wen the lamp-mender. Sects: Qinghe, Beihe, Canglan, Luoyuan, Shouyi, Wanzhou, Hanhui, Dushou, Tianshu. Places: Nine Steps Terrace, Lowwater, the Ashwood Verge, the Broken Kiln, the Lantern Register tower. Institutions: the Bureau of Records, the Lantern order, the Elder's Hall, the Warden's office, the Order of the Recruited, the Scribe.

## Established facts (through ch 24)

- Ning Yan: Body-Forging 3 (ch 1–2) → 9 briefly ch 3 → **4** ch 3–4 → 5 ch 8 → 6 ch 11 → 7 ch 14 (yard losing/work) → 8 ch 22 (4-mile carry + no sleep, tax untouched). Scar sternum to hip ch 3, permanent. Ashwood Nine-Step lost as technique ch 3, habit survives ch 4/14. → 9 ch 26 (locate-work) → **4** ch 36 (through-hand spend, order taken, Nine-Step habit gone). Second scar left shoulder-hip ch 36. Aperture rim cracked (unknown) → cracked through ch 36 permanent.
- Ch 13: furnace column is account, 3-gen rule, settled in kind/service; billed 6 years in air. Du Hong protects from specific number he does not know.
- Ch 14 (deadline): winter drill in 9 days. First deliberate omission between them (asks what else he took, true+incomplete, both let stand). Log foreshadowed.
- Ch 15: past stage 9 needs sect seal/sponsor; servants cannot be sealed. Map absent not locked. Mei Jiu not bad, own account, seal cost.
- Ch 16: layer is office not sky, somebody speaks/writes it; Bureau is visible part. Ning chooses not to know (first time).
- Ch 17: Feng 400 vols, index in head, hand degrading plot fact. Fourth column no heading; Ning Wu in it.
- Ch 18: Warden's marker post (same Vol 4). Wen cart stops, cannot cross (unable not unwilling). Kiln sealed by layer.
- Ch 19: 40 min truly-alone, only this volume, metallic taste. Yuan absent not blocked, returns count wrong.
- Ch 20 onward exact wording: *assessed, reassigned, unreconciled*. Kiln assesses/manufactures.
- Ch 21 irreversible: decides aloud to empty room to take tablet. Tablet single fired plate size of hand, surface like skin (described once, remembered Vol 16).
- Ch 22: removal logged [Removal from sealed ground is logged.]; first thing out 16 years. 7→8 carry, tax untouched noticed. Settlement rises again. She does not ask pay / he never volunteers (pattern to Vol 5).
- Ch 23: Wen will not touch (professional disgust). Tablet made of people / core-stone fired qi. Wears against skin rest Vol 1, never comfortable.
- Ch 24: Yuan can act through her hand (first time in 19 years he picks anything up), slower costs more. Log started in own hand clerks cannot read quickly. Settlement spikes, loses mother's name (name itself, not grief; never says again, never says forgotten). Qiu waits — unresolved.
- Ch 25: log is stolen laundry ledger back pages, deliberately poor hand, wash-code inside learner's strokes, rubbish-camouflage. Seed Vol 4 + Feng fair-hand Wall discovery.
- Ch 26: Body-Forging 8→9 via work + locate. Yuan reads shape of technique off living person, cannot describe in words, only locates. Used Vol 7/13. Winter drill in 3 days met.
- Ch 27: Shen Xiu to report for son's clerkship/inner place; reports ch 34 three days early, never forgiven. Tired/honest not villain.
- Ch 28: Qiu 61, kind, keeps five names in head not paper, gives all five freely. One not servant — plant, Vol 5 payoff.
- Ch 29: Ning Wu *reassigned, signed above furnace authority, in hand Qiu does not know*. Feng recognises Vol 5. Hunt points upward.
- Ch 30: offer real — furnace crew servant-rank inner, bed indoors, stipend, family account written off if she stops asking. Refused then accepted ch 31 as trap-inside-kindness. Second offer logged Vol 4 echo.
- Ch 31: midpoint reversal — Qiu promotes to furnace crew in Hall, approved 11 min, genuine. Bed, stipend, account deferred not settled.
- Ch 32: furnace procedure live = kiln wall word for word, ordinary horror. Two crew friends made, unnamed, both die Vol 2.
- Ch 33: Du Hong inner by her recommendation, sent to report in 3 days, has not. Pallet paper → Feng plan. Dies Vol 2 because of her — start.
- Ch 34: irreversible consent — barred records room, Shen reports early, only way through her, full through-hand cost stated, she says do it. First consented tax; never again until Vol 16.
- Ch 35: Qiu not ordering barring, comes down on own authority, stands between Hall men and door, loses argument on purpose. Not coward/martyr.
- Ch 36: 9→4, aperture cracked through permanent, second scar left shoulder-hip, permanently weaker rest of book. Settlement over half. Tax is withdrawal taking what is on top. Signature Bureau hand nobody recognises; Feng will recognise. Assembly in 19 days, no seal.
- Layer brackets used: ch 6,7,8,22,36, and one in every chapter 37–46. All other chapters zero.
- Records room visited ch 34–35. Broken Kiln interior done 19–21.

## Volume 7 planned — what `outline/volume-07.md` fixed, and what Batch 0001 inherits

**Recorded by the Volume 7 planning phase. No chapter prose was written in it. `outline/series.md` and
`outline/ending.md` were not opened and are unchanged. `outline/volume-07.md` exists and is 786 lines;
`workspace/volume-07-batch-0001/PROMPT.md` is its first drafting card and is the only prompt that phase created.**

**Nothing in this block is measured against ch 361–374, because no Volume 7 chapter exists. Everything below is
the plan, and the first drafting phase's construction budget is measured against the plan and against the chapters
of Volume 6, not against a promise.**

### The fixed items

- **Title, range and goal are fixed by `outline/series.md` §9 and were not re-planned:** *Stronger Things*,
  ch 361–420, sixty chapters.
- **The volume runs day 849 to day 1,090 — 242 days over sixty chapters**, against Volume 6's 228 days over
  sixty-two. **Month-day is `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1`, day 134 was the first of a month, and months are thirty
  days.** Months in Volume 7 begin on days 854 · 884 · 914 · 944 · 974 · 1,004 · 1,034 · 1,064.
- **Four batches, fourteen / fifteen / fifteen / sixteen chapters**, pressure: rooms bigger than she is · what she
  is worth in a room · the reach on purpose · what it costs to be right. **No two consecutive, and none of them a
  tending, a ration, a hiring, a crossing, an audit, an examination, a hunt, a signature, a keeping, a debt, a
  family paper, an ash or a confinement.**

### The day table, every pinned day re-derived under the month rule

| Stage | Ch | Day | Month-day | Interval | Cost, named once |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Nascent Soul 1 (already on the page) | 360 | 845 | the twenty-second of the month beginning 824 | — | spent at ch 360: the surprise does not come back |
| **Nascent Soul 2** | **371** | **886** | the **third** of the month beginning 884 | **41** | **the furnace will not light itself and she cannot light it alone** |
| **Nascent Soul 3** | **383** | **924** | the **eleventh** of the month beginning 914 | **38** | **her hand stops being able to lie** |
| **Nascent Soul 4** | **394** | **960** | the **seventeenth** of the month beginning 944 | **36** | **a thing she reaches cannot be put back where it was** |
| **Nascent Soul 5** | **404** | **999** | the **twenty-sixth** of the month beginning 974 | **39** | **she cannot be given anything** |
| **Nascent Soul 6** | **412** | **1,037** | the **fourth** of the month beginning 1,034 | **38** | **she cannot be paid** |
| **Nascent Soul 7** | **420** | **1,076** | the **thirteenth** of the month beginning 1,064 | **39** | **the reach will not go out of a room she has not been asked into** |

- **Six stages in about eight months, real intervals of 41, 38, 36, 39, 38 and 39 days, no skipped breakthrough,
  no tribulation.** Volume 6 ran seven stages in about seven months at 38, 36, 38, 37, 37, 32 and 27. **The 41 is
  the longest interval in either volume and the ch 371 chapter is required to say why it is that long** — the board
  was bare, the season had not turned, and a stage needs material she had no way to pay for.
- **None of the six costs is a limb, a hold, a count of breath or a missing finger**, so none collides with the
  body's standing injuries. **Three of the six are not losses at all** — the hand that cannot lie, the gift that
  cannot be taken and the pay that cannot be entered are three doors closing rather than three things taken away,
  and **two of those three do not touch her body in any way. None undoes a Volume 6 closure:** the furnace is not
  un-finished, it simply stops lighting itself, which is a different fact and a worse one; the heavy end stays
  closed; the bar stays a two-handed tool; the refusal at ch 358 stands in its book and nothing takes it out.
- **The series-table cost of Nascent Soul — *she can no longer be surprised by a person* — is already spent at
  ch 360 and is not re-spent, re-explained or softened anywhere in Volume 7.**

### Bracket budget for Volume 7, honest and checkable by grep

**Six in sixty. One at each Nascent Soul stage, at ch 371, 383, 394, 404, 412 and 420, and nowhere else.** The far-side
house form is Volume 6's, character for character, with only the realm and the number changed:

[Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Nascent Soul, stage two, is held.]

**Volume 6 spent six in sixty-two and none at Nascent Soul 1, because the handover was entered by hand and that
silence was the point. The form does not change at any of Volume 7's six.** Ch 394 is the one chapter that hears
the last four words of it and finds they have stopped being true — there is no county within nine days of road —
and she thinks it, and the layer does not change.

### The announcement rule, re-derived for Volume 7

**The rule did not change. The listeners did.** The layer still announces; the sound still goes into the ground,
because there is nobody inside a bound to be told and no county book to take it. **What changed is that the rooms
past the mill have learned to look.** A stage used to reach a strip about three days later, in the fine hand, at a
gate post. **From ch 371 it happens in daylight in front of two or three people on that ground**, and after that it
is a thing discussed in rooms she is not in, at figures she cannot check.

**The consequence that drives the volume: from ch 371 she is entered as a body and not only as a column, and that
is the reason she is the weakest thing in every room she walks into.** Nobody announces it. A man at a bar looks at
her and works out where she goes in the order.

### The protagonist, and the second rung of *Breaking*

- **`outline/series.md` §4 gives him Hand across Volumes 6–8.** Volume 6 took the first volume of it. Volume 7
  takes the second half and the second rung.
- **The second rung is the direction of a sending.** At ch 402 the holding is used, by him, on purpose, for the
  first time, to move an office. **What breaks is the assumption that a line on that ground can only be read
  downward.** The Wall is not opened, the Order is not on the page, and no office above the copying floor or the
  second office is named or entered.
- **The third frame scene is pinned at ch 401**, away from her hearing, in his own frame. **He has been on the
  page that way exactly twice in six volumes, at ch 272 and ch 338.** The third makes him **answerable**, which is
  a third thing and worse than being sendable, because an answer can be quoted.
- **Hand is spent once (ch 402) and refused once, out loud (ch 419).** Each spending is told before spent, out of
  her foundation, and does not come back. **Nothing in Volume 7 kills anybody.**
- **Merit settles three times** — once in Batch 0001, once in Batch 0003, once in Batch 0004 — each on a listed
  piece of work cleared past the mill, each share told before spent. **No figure of what is left of him is stated
  on any page**, and the last figure in the manuscript is still *about six weeks* at ch 236.
- **Ledger is never ordered, held, priced, refused or thought about**, and `grep -i Ledger` returns **0** across
  all sixty-two files of Volume 6 and returns 0 on Volume 7's page. It took two close repairs at ch 306 and ch 319
  to make Volume 6's zero true rather than approximately true, so this is measured and not asserted.

### Two irreversible decisions

1. **Ch 388, hers.** She says a person's name out loud in a room and asks for something — the first time in ten
   months she has asked anybody for anything. It is entered in a room that keeps things and it cannot be unsaid.
2. **Ch 417, hers, and it is the volume's own.** She declines to know what the fine hand says about her. A denial
   is an entry, so she goes on not knowing, forever, on purpose. **Nothing is written anywhere, no book fails to
   shut and no circuit breaks — the bill is entirely hers.**

**Volume 6's break was a circuit broken (ch 344), Volume 6's refusal was a permanent entry in a building she
cannot reach (ch 358), and Volume 7's refusal is an answer withheld (ch 417): three acts in three consecutive
volumes, three different shapes, none a restatement of another.**

### The structural answer, continued and not paid

**Six mouths, six new people, none of them one of Volume 6's seven, four or five sentences each, never a speech:**
a carter (ch 365) · a gate man (ch 379) · **Rao**, the column-keeper (ch 391) · a woman who drives clay (ch 398) ·
a copying man one floor below Rao (ch 409) · **Geng**, who runs the lifting floor (ch 415). **The tenth part is not
given and nothing proves the crews wrong.** The origin answer (Volume 11) and the moral answer (Volumes 15–16) are
not previewed, and the surface answer is not re-voiced.

### Three new named people, chosen so none collides with an existing one

**Rao** (keeps the columns on the third floor, sixteen years — the midpoint's reversal) · **Dai** (whose job it is
to read the fine hand — offers once, refused once, right both times) · **Geng** (runs the lifting floor — strong,
correct, and a block short for a week). **`Mei`, `Xie` and `Du` were rejected as bare surnames because the
manuscript already has Mei Jiu, Lu Mei, Xie Shuo, Xie Qiao and Du Hong inside the bound.** **The keeper with the
paste pot stays unnamed**, as he was in Volume 6, and is given the heaviest scene in Volume 7.

### Construction budget carried into Volume 7 — measured against Volume 6, not promised

Every figure in the right-hand column of `outline/volume-07.md` § *The construction budget, carried from Volume 6
and re-measured* is a Volume 6 measurement or a stated Volume 7 allowance. **The two that bind hardest:**

- **The bracket budget: six in sixty, at ch 371, 383, 394, 404, 412 and 420, and zero anywhere else.** Volume 6's
  was six in sixty-two at ch 310, 321, 332, 342, 352 and 357 with none at ch 360. **A budget that records zero while
  instances stand is the failure this manuscript has caught itself making twice, and a budget that records a count
  where there is none is the same lie with the sign flipped — which is how the Batch 0004 `sending: 7` row was
  wrong and had to be corrected.**
- **The `nobody` family: nobody 88 · no one 4 · not one 50 · total 142** across Volume 6's sixty-two chapters,
  about 2.3 a chapter. **The three intensifiers are the family and they total 142; `family`/`families` is a
  separate word and stands at 6.** *(An earlier record in this file, and the copies inherited by
  `outline/volume-07.md` and `workspace/volume-07-batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, read the last figure as `family 142`,
  which is a transcription of `total 142` and not a measurement. Corrected at the planning review.)*
  **That is the one measured number in Volume 6 above the book's own guidance** and Volume 7's
  allowance is **under sixty across sixty chapters**, which is about one a chapter. The true count is written into
  the continuity block whatever it is.

**Volume 6's zeros, re-measured at this planning phase against `chapters/volume-06/` and unchanged:**
`reconciled` **0** · `plate` **0** · `licence` **0** · `license` **0** · `clearance` **0** ·
`countersignature` **0** · `kiln-born` **0** · `MEMBER ABSENT` **0** · `Ledger` **0** · `tribulation` **0** ·
`Feng` **0** · `nobody` **88**. **360 `#` headings across the manuscript and zero collisions**, against which
Volume 7's sixty titles must be unique. **Wei Ran appears once in Volume 6, at ch 302, and Volume 7 gives her at
most two cards and nothing more than that.**

### What Batch 0001 inherits, in one paragraph

**Day 849, the twenty-sixth of a month beginning on day 824. At a gate and a shed three miles east of a mill whose
wheel has not turned.** Nascent Soul 1, taken by hand and not by sound on day 845 with no bracket, and a reach that
began once and does not come back. Four fingers' grip on one hand, the right arm open half the time, eight of eleven
holds, five counts of breath on a loaded reach, the bar needing two hands, the licence never opened, the plate worn
and undescribed. **No wages, no season, no booking; a gate at one copper a day, bread five days in seven, about half
a box of clay, and the board at the works bare until the season turns. A furnace that lights itself.** Three books
that can read her, one of them a copy made upward in a hand she cannot read; a refusal entered in a counter's book
in a building she cannot reach; her own book on a stone under a keeper's ladder at a post. He is readable, findable,
held and sendable, and Volume 7 opens being sent for at ch 402 and pays it out on the page.

### What Batch 0001 must not do, pinned

**One bracket, at ch 371, and no other.** No Hand — first spent ch 402, refused ch 419, both outside this batch. No
frame scene. **Being sent for is not opened, foreshadowed in his mouth, or had happen off-page.** No refusal — the
volume's is ch 417. No office named, no word of the Order, no Wei Ran, no Feng, no Ning Wu, no payment and no
listing. The register is not read, the plate is not described, MEMBER ABSENT is carried and not raised, the year of
non-forgiveness is not mentioned once, and no villain is created.

## Volume 7 planning — close review repair

**Six findings from `logs/volume-07-planning.review.log`, all applied. Every figure was re-measured against
`chapters/volume-06/` before it was written back. No chapter prose was touched, no beat, day, title, batch
range, pinned chapter, mouth, stage, bracket or irreversible step moved, and no Volume 7 chapter exists or was
invented.**

- **The `nobody` family row, in four files** (`workspace/volume-07-batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, `outline/volume-07.md`,
  this file, and `state/current.md`), read **`family 142`**. It is **`total 142`** — `nobody` 88, `no one` 4,
  `not one` 50 — and **`family`/`families` measures 6**. The mistranscription mattered: the `under 14 across
  fourteen` allowance is the drafter`s main defence against the bare-intensifier construction, and it was
  specified against a number 24x too high. The rows now name the three intensifiers the allowance governs and
  give both figures.
- **`vouch` is 6, not 4** — ch 357 x4 (lines 39, 79, 105 x2) and **ch 329 line 41**, *you have been vouched once
  already in a building with a stove*. `grep -c` returns 3 at ch 357 because line 105 carries two occurrences.
- **`send for` 4 / `sent for` 2 / `sending` 1**, against a recorded 1 / 0 / 0. At ch 301:65, 336:71, 337:69,
  338:133 (twice on the line), 338:167 and 355:59.
- **All-caps standalone lines is 2, not 0** — ch 338 lines 47 and 51, both `HELD.`. These are the layer`s own
  word set alone as a deliberate beat, not written matter in a hand, and they were never in the class of the seven
  the Volume 6 close repaired; all seven are sentence case and stay that way. The Batch 0001 prompt now carries
  the warning at the point where it orders the drafter to extend into ch 330–345, because ch 338 is the chapter
  that breaks its own rule and would otherwise be inherited as precedent.
- **`outline/volume-07.md:558` said `ch 378`** where its own structural-answer table at `:404` and five state
  files say **`379`**. One digit corrected to the majority and to the outline`s own table.
- **`outline/volume-07.md` is 786 lines**, not the 784 recorded here.
- **Forbidden-string greps now carry word boundaries.** Bare `Scribe` returns a false positive at ch 303 inside
  *described*, and `plate` and `Ledger` will do the same; the Volume 6 close was caught by this on `Ledger`.

**Not touched, and verified as correct rather than missed.** `state/continuity.md` lines 6008-6010 record `vouch`
4, `sending` 0, `send for` 1 and `sent for` 0. **Those rows are scoped to Batch 0004`s fifteen files** and
re-measured across ch 346-360 they return exactly those numbers. The findings were against the Volume 6-wide
rows in the prompt and the outline, not against these, and repairing them would have made a right measurement
wrong.

**Everything the reviewer listed as verified stays verified**: all day arithmetic under `((day - 134) mod 30) + 1`
(845, 848, 849, 899, 886, 924, 960, 999, 1,037, 1,076 and the intervals 41/38/36/39/38/39), the six bracketed layer
lines at ch 310, 321, 332, 342, 352 and 357 with none at ch 360, the fourteen forbidden strings at 0, bold and
blockquote and code fence and table and horizontal rule at 0, straight quotes 0/0, the seven retired phrases at 0,
the longest paragraph at 100 words (ch 309) with 13 in the 91-100 band, and 360 `#` headings manuscript-wide with
zero collisions. The fixed quantities, the day table, the batch ranges and the pinned ch 371 card all survive.

## Established facts, Volume 7 Batch 0001 (ch 361–374) — ROOMS BIGGER THAN SHE IS

**Fourteen chapters, 27,114 words of body prose, average ~1,937, day 849 to day 899.** **ONE bracketed layer line,
at ch 371 — bracket 1 of Volume 7's six in sixty.** Chapter titles and section headings excluded. Drafted against
`outline/volume-07.md` and against `workspace/volume-07-batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, which is this batch's card. **The
prompt's corrected handoff was right and the page followed it: the batch opens on day 849, the twenty-sixth, at the
gate, the day after ch 360 closed — not on the stage day and not on the day the strip came.**

Per file, body prose: 361 1,957 · 362 1,984 · 363 1,900 · 364 1,827 · 365 1,953 · 366 1,868 · 367 1,869 · 368 2,172 ·
369 1,885 · 370 2,063 · 371 1,963 · 372 1,899 · 373 1,913 · 374 1,861. **All fourteen inside the 1,800–2,700 guide,
none under the 1,300 floor and none above the ceiling.** Volume 7 file set after this batch: **27,114 words over
fourteen chapters.**

### The batch in one sentence

She carried a strip she could not read to a gate that paid a copper a day, watched a reading leave the only room on
nine days of road that could fetch anybody for it and go east nine miles, found the board full and every line on it a
line her right hand could not take, stood in a yard of nine men and was priced as a body in about two seconds, had a
bar go across her on a bank and stopped it with her own furnace, took her second stage in daylight in front of two
men, and lost the fire that had lit itself every morning for four months.

### The clock and the day table — every pinned day verified under `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1`

**Months in this batch: the month beginning day 824 runs to day 853, the month beginning day 854 runs to day 883,
and the month beginning day 884 runs to day 913.**

| 367 | **864** | the 11th — **the season turns** | It Comes Back Full |

  Day 899 is the sixteenth of the same month.** Day 854 is the first of the month that the cart turned on.

### The money table, the whole of it, and how it moves in this batch

| | Entering the batch (day 849) | At the last page (day 899) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Gate at Pei’s | five coppers a reach, **a fifth of a reach, one copper a day**, taken out with a short iron out of the gate house because the bar takes two hands | **the same, one copper a day**, and **the drain has gone from four feet to three and a half** and Pei says so out loud on the sixteenth |
| Listed work | the board at the works bare for about a month | **full since the eleventh**, eleven lines, every one dressed stone priced by the ton, **and she can take none of them** |
| The yard at the turn | — | **a length three days in seven**, paid in a cup of something hot from a woman who has never asked her name. **Nothing in money. Nothing on a board. No name of hers anywhere in the yard** |

Thirteen days of gate at a copper a day is thirteen in; one trip to the store is eight out; five coppers in the fold in the inside of the door frame at the end of them** |

- **The gate rate does not move in this batch and the reason is on the page at ch 361**: the bar takes two hands, so
  the gate has a short iron in it and no bar, and a fifth of a reach is what four feet of drain with a turn of an iron
  comes to. It was a third of a reach and one and a half coppers before Golden Core eight; that half copper went out of
  her life at ch 353 and did not come back here.
- **A month is thirty coppers and thirty is the whole of what this ground can pay her.** She works it out standing up
  on the twenty-seventh (ch 362): bread twenty, a third of a sack three, and **seven coppers over for a month**, and a
  third of a sack is three of the seven, and four coppers is two days of bread.
- **No board line is taken in this batch. No listing. No wages. No season ending on a date.** The pay question does
  not turn until Nascent Soul 6 at ch 412.

| The books she cannot see | three, one a copy made upward in a fine hand | **three, and the column in one of them has two lines at the foot of it where it had four for four months** |
| The reading | a strip in her coat with a word at the head of it she cannot read, and a date she had lived through | **the reading has left the room.** A strip went east on the first at a quarter past the sixth hour and did not come back on the cart that takes the empty trays up |
| The strip | folded in two against the oilcloth | **the same strip. It did not come back.** It is somewhere nine miles east and she is not going east of the turn |
| The furnace | lights itself at about the second hour, every morning, for four months | **will not light itself, and she cannot light it alone** |
| The yard | — | **nine men on a board where four would do. A rule in a hand. A hedge line closed and off** |

### Nascent Soul 2 — ch 371, day 886, the third of the month beginning 884

Taken standing, in the open, on the flat past the last hedge at about the seventh hour, **with nine men from the yard
on a bank about sixty feet long and two of them nine feet off her**. **Forty-one days after Nascent Soul 1 at day 845,
and the chapter says why it is the longest interval in the volume**: the board was bare, the season had not turned,
and a stage needs material she had no way to pay for.

[Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Nascent Soul, stage two, is held.]

**It is bracket 1 of Volume 7's six in sixty, and the form does not change at any of the six.**

**Cost, named once, permanent: the furnace will not light itself and she cannot light it alone.** Not a limb, not a
hold, not a count of breath and not a missing finger. **A stage cannot be built by one pair of hands.** She tries it
nine times over about three hours with clay and with ash and with her hands flat on the brick and with her hands held
four inches off it, and it comes up a finger's width and stops every time.

**What this batch took out of her body, and it is on the page and measurable:** her hands go cold. They had been warm
for four months — warm the way a furnace is warm — and they went cold within about a moment of the bar stopping at
the third hour on the second of a month, and they are cold on the last page.

### The bank, the load, and the furnace — ch 370, day 885, the 2nd of the month beginning 884

The batch's physical-risk card and the set-up for the cost, on a pinned date, in the same chapter family as the stage.

- **A bank at about a mile and a quarter east of the mill, out on the flat past the last hedge**, a face about four
  feet, clay under stone, nine men on it, taken by the yard four miles east at the turn.
- **The bar goes.** Not a slip: it comes out of the face and comes down the low side with four pieces of the heavy
  stone on it, and she is on the low side because she has come round the end of the face to fill a barrow with the
  loose. About a ton, about nine inches of air under her shoulders, and the nearest man four feet off and two seconds
  away.
- **What stops it is the furnace in the shed.** She lifts — the thing she learned at a bank nine days west from a
  woman with a scar across the back of both hands — and it comes up out of the ground under her ribs and goes under
  what is on top of her, and it is not her arms and it is not her hands and nothing about it can be looked at. **The
  bar stops a finger's width** and two strong men take the rest of it in two seconds.
- **The fire does not come up on the next morning, and it does not come up on any of the thirteen days after it.**
- **Nothing in this batch kills anybody.**

### Merit settles once, on a listed clearing, told before spent

- **Ch 370, the second hour of the first of a month.** A hedge line at the east end of the yard, four hundred yards,
  by the length, one day, **closed and checked and off on the twentieth.** Done by her, with her hands, off nobody's
  book, for no money.
- **Not a death.** There is no death in it and there has not been one in her accounting since the crossing.
- **The share is six days and four hours and it is said out loud before it is spent, in his words, twice.** He tells
  her he has not said that number out loud in four years and he says it to her because she is going to spend it
  before she has had a night with it and he would rather she spent it having heard it.
- **She spends it on nothing.** Not on a hold, not on a bar, not on her hours, not on the reach. It goes out of the
  ground under her into the floor of a shed, out of her foundation, and does not come back.
- **The price is the one nobody can name or measure, and the page shows it rather than measures it: the ring stops
  coming up the following morning.**
- **No Hand is spent in this batch. `Ledger` returns zero across all fourteen files.**

### Yuan — rationed, present tense, buying nothing and advising nothing

Utterances outside the second hour of ch 370: **ch 362 (one) · ch 364 (two) · ch 370 (four) · ch 371 (none) ·
ch 369 (two)**. **Ch 370 carries the share, told twice, plus the four sentences about it; every one is in the present
tense and none of it buys anything.** Nothing at ch 361, 363, 365, 366, 367, 368, 372, 373 or 374 except the four
sentences at the second hour on the sixteenth of ch 374, which are the arrangement and nothing more.

**He is readable without her and it is on the page and it costs nothing to say: at ch 362 he knows she has counted a
month three times and that she counted one of them wrong.** **No figure of what is left of him is stated on any page
in this batch.** The date of the morning he died is not given back. The unnamed state is not given back. **Being sent
for is not opened, not foreshadowed in his mouth, and does not happen to anybody else.** No frame scene.

### The structural answer — mouth one of six, at ch 365, in the carter’s mouth

**First of Volume 7's six mouths and six new people.** A carter with a chain over his cart bed who drives the long
road east:

“I have been turned back at the end of it four hundred times. That is not a thing that happens to me. That is the
ordinary end of that road.” · “Turning back is what people pay for. They pay for the going out. They pay for the
stopping.” · “There is no part in the middle. And I have never asked what is up there, and a man who asks finds out
that somebody has an answer.”

Four or five sentences, in short sentences, not a speech, and **the tenth part is not given and nothing proves the
crews wrong.** It is a different statement from Volume 6's seven mouths and it is a different man: Volume 6's ch 353
carter drove past the end of the line and watched it from this side, and this one drives the long road east and has
been turned back at the end of it.

### The Hunter — on the page, ch 369 only

**Nine feet off the cart track two hundred yards west of the works, on the twenty-second of a month at about the
seventh hour, notebook shut under his arm, no other paper of any kind.** He buys nothing and he asks nothing, and she
asks **him**, which is what she has done since ch 347 and is the only handle there has ever been on him.

**What she asks:** what a room on this ground is for. **What he answers, in the ordinary voice, at no cost to either
of them:** *A room is where a thing is put so that four people can agree about it later.* Then: *I am still here, and
I am still buying nothing, and I am not going to tell you why, and you are not going to ask me.*

**His request stays pending. A writ is not issued to a field agent. The line’s own people still do not know she
exists.** Nothing about his paper, his order or his brother is raised. He is not named.

### Untouched, unraised, and still true at ch 374

The register still reads *reconciled* — `reconciled` returns **zero** and **nothing in this batch reads it** · the
Clay Plate worn against her ribs and **not described** (`plate` returns zero) · the relicensed licence **never
opened** and never referred to (`licence` and `license` both return zero) · MEMBER ABSENT carried and never raised
(**returns zero**) · **the year of non-forgiveness not mentioned once in fourteen chapters** · Shen Xiu unforgiven ·
*kiln-born* zero · what became of the eleven not raised · the four months erased in Chapter 3 never named · the ch
150 refusal not softened · **no office named at all, in any mouth, and nothing above the copying floor entered,
described or named** · the surface answer not re-voiced · **the claim not reopened** · Hou Sanliang paid, never met,
not on the page · Ning Wu does not appear and Ning Bo does not appear · **Wei Ran does not appear** · Feng does not
appear and no second Lung and no second appointment · no harem and nothing a romance · **she never says her mother's
name** · **no new enemy and no named villain** · **not one word of the Order is on the page** · `Ledger` returns zero ·
**nothing in this batch kills anybody** · **no warrant, no demand, no confinement, no keeping, no debt, no family
paper, no ash, no ration, no hiring, no tending, no crossing, no audit, no examination, no signature** — the pressure
of this batch is being the smallest thing in a room, and nothing else.

### Live at the last page of Batch 0001, going into Batch 0002

- **The furnace will not light itself and she cannot light it alone, and there is no person on nine days of road she
  is entitled to ask.** She has worked out that the one person whose hands she could put in that corner is the man
  with the paste pot at a post on the mill road, who cannot read her book and would not tell anybody, and that the
  counting house is about two miles short of his own ladder on a road she walks four or five times a month.
  **She has not said it to anybody.** **She may not ask until ch 388.**
- **A strip went east on the first of a month at a quarter past the sixth hour and did not come back on the cart that
  takes the empty trays up.** It is about nine miles east and she is not going east of the turn.
- **A column nine days of road away has two lines at the foot of it where it had four for four months**, and the
  change came about three days after the strip went east, and she cannot read a word of either shape.
- **Every stage she takes from ch 371 onward is a public fact on this ground.** Two men on a bank at the seventh hour
  on the third of a month have seen one happen.
- **She is the smallest thing in every yard she walks into and the reason is on the page: a yard prices a body, and
  every office she has been entered in has her column in it, and there is nowhere on nine days of road that has her
  arm on a line.**
- **The board is full and she can take none of it, the gate pays one copper a day at three and a half feet, and the
  only work open to her on that ground is unpaid length work at a yard and reading that nobody pays for.**

## Construction budget — Volume 7 Batch 0001 (ch 361–374), measured by grep, not promised

**Every figure below was run against the fourteen files after they were written. Nine categories of fault were found by
measurement while drafting and all nine were cut before the batch was saved; they are recorded, because a budget that
only ever reports clean is not a budget.**

| Construction | Result across ch 361–374 |

**Nine categories of fault were found by measurement while drafting and all nine were cut before the batch was saved:
four instances of a named weekday, two of the retired *about four seconds*, twenty-two of the *nobody* family, one
byte-identical duplicate paragraph, seven paragraphs between 94 and 103 words, straight apostrophes in ten files, and
two chapter titles that were not names. No beat, day-table position, title, turn, midpoint, climax, irreversible
decision, bracket or stage moved in any of them, and no chapter in `chapters/volume-01/` … `chapters/volume-06/` was
opened.**

**One discrepancy between the batch prompt and the page, resolved in favour of the prompt's rule and recorded.** The
prompt pinned ch 365 at days 853–856 and named three of them. The page carries **853–855**, which are the thirtieth,
the first and the second, and ch 366 opens on the third. **Every other pinned item in the prompt is on the page at the
month-day the prompt's own rule gives it.**

**One thing the prompt did not have and the page built: the season's date.** The board is bare until the season turns
and the prompt did not say when. **The page turns it on the eleventh of a month — day 864 — and the eleven lines it
comes back with are all dressed stone priced by the ton**, which is what makes the batch's third beat work: the board
fills, every line on it is a line her right hand cannot take, and the only work left narrows to reading, which nobody
on that ground will pay for.

## Volume 7 Batch 0001 — REVIEW REPAIR (applied after `logs/volume-07-batch-0001.review.log`)

**Six findings, three of them the reviewer's actionable three. The batch was not restarted, no chapter in
`chapters/volume-01/` … `chapters/volume-06/` was opened, no beat, day-table position, turn, midpoint, climax,
irreversible decision, bracket or stage moved, and `outline/volume-07.md` was not re-planned.**

### 1. The money did not reconcile at ch 374 — FIXED, and it was a real arithmetic error

The page had her earn thirteen coppers over thirteen gate days, go to the store twice at eight coppers a trip, take a
fifth of a sack of clay, **spend nothing**, and finish on **twenty-three coppers** — which is also ch 362's *twenty-three
of thirty*, a different figure from a different sum, reused.

**The store's price is fixed three times in the batch and always the same: four loaves for eight coppers (ch 362, in the
storekeeper's mouth), bread for five days, eight coppers (ch 366 and ch 372).** Two trips out of thirteen coppers is
sixteen out of thirteen in, and the clay is dug out of a drain and is not bought.

**The page now says one trip, on the seventh, and five coppers at the end**, and it says the sum out loud in her own
arithmetic — *thirteen days, thirteen coppers, one trip west at eight, five in the fold in the inside of the door frame* —
so the figure is checkable by the reader and it checks. **She enters the thirteen days with eight days' bread (ch 371,
ch 372) and buys five more, which is the whole of what she eats.**

**A second figure in the same chapter did not count and was cut.** *The List She Did Not Say Out Loud* named seven slots
and then said six people, because *the man behind the counter at the mill road* was the same man as *the man behind the
counter* two items earlier, and the sum *came out in five*. **The list is six, the duplicate is gone, and the sum now
comes out in six.** A book whose whole aesthetic is arithmetic cannot have two places where a reader's count and the
narrator's count come out different.

### 2. Twelve of fourteen chapters sat below the pacing floor — FIXED

**23,101 → 27,114 words of body prose, average ~1,650 → ~1,937. All fourteen chapters now sit inside the 1,800–2,700
guide.** The gap was a drafting artefact, not a plan: the batch had fourteen cards and a writer who built a chapter to
the card and stopped. **The expansion added scene material inside the existing scenes and changed no card.** Sixty-one new
paragraphs: the cold out of a four-foot cut and the hasp on an unlocked box (361) · the cold room behind a store counter
she has thought about for six months (362) · a worn stair, a brush worn to a stub, nine men at a counter and four on a
floor (363) · the fourth and fifth times she saw a reading leave a room, and why they were not the same thing (364) ·
the loaf she carried in her coat for three miles, the elms, and the shape of the road drawn out on the boards (365) ·
two hours on a shoulder with her hands empty, a carter who does not slow down, and the ninth hour for a cart that takes
nine (366) · reading a board up a column instead of along a line, and the anger that was not at him (367) · nine men who
have seen a person once (369) · a third thing to try at the fourth hour, holding her fingers against the brick until the
two temperatures agreed (371) · catching herself in the doorway with the lump already in her hand, the hasp that is never
locked, and *she had not been making a fire, she had been doing a thing beside a fire* (372) · a barrow pushed four miles
up a made road and brought back with the clay still in it (373) · the six days east, the boy putting the stick down
through the rails, and the cup of something hot she could not make an opening out of (374).

### 3. `about` had become a tic, and the budget did not measure it — FIXED

**312 → 197.** Detail and method in the budget row above. **The reviewer's larger point was structural, and it is the part
that matters: the batch prompt names `about…` as the first construction to watch and the budget measured it nowhere.**
`workspace/volume-07-batch-0002/PROMPT.md` has been given a measured row for it.

### 4. Twelve eight-word paragraph openings recurred — FIXED to two planted pairs

Ten of twelve recast, listed in the budget row. **The two that remain are on the page on purpose**: the Hunter's sentence
said once and repeated in her head eleven lines later (369), and *The fire did not come up on the …*, which opens the run
of thirteen days at ch 372 and closes it at ch 374.

**A third repeat was not a repeat at all but a rule breach, and the repair found it.** The prompt says the cost is
*named once and never re-explained in any later chapter*. **Ch 374 was quoting it — *A stage cannot be built by one pair
of hands* — four chapters after it was named at ch 371. Ch 374 now pays the cost instead of saying the line**, which is
what that chapter was for.

### 5. Six titles opened on the word *What* — FIVE RENAMED

Listed in the budget row. **One *What* is left, at ch 371, and a table of contents with one *What* in it is a table of
contents.** All five replacements are names; none restates the scene, the hour, the day, an age or the weather.

### 6. `week` was reported as 0/0/0 and was not 0 — FIXED IN THE BUDGET, NOT IN THE PROSE

**Sixteen ordinary durations stand in the batch — *a week's bread* twice, *twice a week*, *a week later*, *two weeks
back*, *three weeks*, *six weeks*, *a fortnight* — and none of them is a named week.** The page was right; the budget's
row was measuring the wrong thing and reporting a clean 0/0/0. **The row is now two columns, name and duration, so the
next batch cannot hide behind it again.** One duration was recast anyway, at ch 370, where *for a week* was also wrong
arithmetic and is now *and she did not think about it again*.

### What did not change

The plot, the cards, the day table, the pinned days, the bracket and its position, the midpoint at ch 368, the physical
risk at ch 370, the irreversible decision at ch 371, the merit settlement, the Hunter's single appearance, and every
thing still open at ch 374. **The batch is still fourteen chapters, still one bracket, still no office named above the
copying floor, still no weekday and no named month anywhere, still nothing killed.**

## Established facts, Volume 7 Batch 0002 (ch 375–389) — WHAT SHE IS WORTH IN A ROOM

**Fifteen chapters, 28,296 words of body prose, average ~1,886, day 900 to day 950.** *(The 27,966 / ~1,864 this line carried was stale and is corrected here and everywhere else it stood; see *Volume 7 Batch 0002 — word-count correction* below.*)* **ONE bracketed layer line, at
ch 383 — bracket 2 of Volume 7's six in sixty, character for character in the far-side house form.** Chapter titles
and section headings excluded. Drafted against `outline/volume-07.md` and against
`workspace/volume-07-batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, which was this batch's card. **The prompt's corrected handoff was right and
the page followed it: the batch opens on day 900, the seventeenth, the day after ch 374 closed — not on the first, not
on a stage day, and not on a pay-day, and this ground has no pay-day.**

Per file, body prose, **re-derived at the Batch 0004 review and not the figures this batch recorded**: 375 2,007 · 376 1,929 · 377 1,895 · 378 1,845 · 379 1,881 · 380 1,824 · 381 1,819 · 382 1,903 ·
383 1,818 · 384 1,862 · 385 1,792 · 386 1,837 · 387 1,886 · 388 1,835 · 389 2,163. **Fourteen of the fifteen are inside
the 1,800–2,700 guide and ch 385 is under it at 1,792; none under the 1,300 floor and none above the ceiling.** Volume 7 file set after this batch: **55,080 words of
body prose over twenty-nine chapters.**

### The batch in one sentence

She worked out that a man who keeps a board has to fetch his own strips, burned a man who was owed nothing by saying
*yes* out loud when she could have said four words and did not, walked east of the turn for the first time in her life,
stood at the top of a stair in a room full of open books and was told in eleven words that there was no place there for
her, took her third stage in that room, found that her hand had stopped being able to lie, was offered a line of her
own for ten coppers and could neither pay it nor take it, went into a four-foot cut that came in on her and cost her
the last of her fist, and then said a woman's name out loud in a room and asked her for four hours a month and gave
her own name to get it.

### The clock and the day table — every pinned day verified under `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1`

**Months in this batch: the month beginning day 884 runs to day 913, the month beginning day 914 runs to day 943, and
the month beginning day 944 runs to day 973.**

### The money table, the whole of it, and how it moves in this batch

Pei looks at it, says nothing the third time, and goes for his cup |
| Bread | two coppers a loaf, four loaves for five days at eight, store five miles west | **the same, and the regime settles: a trip on the sixth, the fourteenth, the twenty-second and the thirtieth of a month.** Seven coppers in seven days and eight out of bread was the standing arithmetic and it has been eating a stock since she came past this mill; the stock is gone and the arithmetic has come out level |
| Clay | four barrow-loads against the outside wall and a fifth of a sack in a barrow | **six barrow-loads against the wall and a seventh standing on its handles, and not one lump of it has ever been in a ring that lit** |
| The yard at the turn | a length three days in seven, a cup of something hot, no name of hers anywhere | **the same, and a length three days in seven, and the cup, and the woman in the office end has still never asked her name and has put one into her hands six times in three weeks** |
| The third floor | — | **no money at all in it. A margin correction on that floor is four coppers and she has eight. A line entered at the counter in the seasonal book is ten coppers, which is a day at that counter and ten days at Pei's gate** |
| Purse | **five coppers in the fold in the inside of the door frame** | **fifteen coppers, and the sum is said out loud and done twice on the boards: ten at the end of the thirtieth, eleven on the first, thirty-six days at the gate after that at a copper a day, four trips west at eight, fifteen at the last page** |

- **No board line is taken in this batch. No listing. No wages. No season ending on a date.** The pay question does
  not turn until Nascent Soul 6 at ch 412, and **NS 3 closes a different door, not the pay one.**
- **Every figure in this batch comes out of Batch 0001's price table.** One copper a day · five coppers a reach · two
  coppers a loaf and eight for four · three coppers a third of a sack of clay · four coppers for a margin correction on
  the third floor (new, stated once and on the page) · ten coppers for a day at the counter (new, stated once, and
  derived from the gate rate rather than guessed).

### The construction the batch is built on, and how it moves

| | Entering (day 899) | At the last page (day 950) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The rooms | a counter and a stove, a stair to a copying floor, and the yard of nine men at the turn | **a counter and a stove, a copying floor with one man on the top step, a third floor with nine steps and a landing with a turn in it, forty books open on a table, and a stair book at the north end** |
| The reading | a strip in her coat she cannot read, and a column nine days east with two lines off it | **the same strip, taken up nine steps four times a month and put on the table. Both threads carried and neither touched** |
| The fire | will not light itself and cannot be lit alone | **it is not a place. It comes up wherever she is with another living thing in the room, and the place it comes up in is whichever room she is standing in, and she cannot choose it and it cannot be taken out of the shed** |
| Her hands | cold since the second of a month, and she had stopped expecting them to be warm | **cold on every road between the shed and that table, and warm in that table's room when a woman is in it, about a hundred yards short of a gate on the mill road where they go cold again every time** |
| Her hand | four fingers and a thumb, and able to write whatever she tells it | **four fingers and a thumb, and a line she puts in ink in her own hand is a thing she means. It wrote a four five times over when it was set to write a seven** |
| Her arm | open half the time | **open half the time, and it went into the other thing for two seconds on the twenty-first in a ditch, and she cannot close her right hand at all** |

### The second pair of hands — ch 378, day 914, the first of a month

**The batch's engine and the card that pays NS 2's cost without ever saying it.**

- **She works out at the gate on the seventeenth that a man who keeps a board has to fetch his own strips**, and that he
  fetches them at the counting house on the first of a month with the cart, because that is the only morning in the
  month anything comes down that road. **She does not go to the post.** The prompt's tension is resolved the way the
  page had already half resolved it: the counting house is on the mill road, she walks it four or five times a month,
  and a man whose trade is strips has an ordinary reason to be standing in a gateway on his own road.
- **On the first of a month at about the sixth hour, in a low building with nine men in it, a man about fifty-five with
  a paste pot and a rag gets down off a high stool with his left hand flat on the floor, and the floor comes up about a
  second and a half before it is under his hand.**
- **She had four words available and she did not say them**, because a word said at the sixth hour in a low building
  with nine men in it goes where four people can agree about it later, and she cannot read the hand.
- **He asks her one question — *did you do that* — and she says the word.** *Yes.* **That is the batch's worst thing
  happening to her at the hands of a person who is right:** he looks at her arm for two seconds, the way a man looks at
  a thing he is working out where it goes in an order, says he has got a bundle two miles up the road and has not got
  a hand that closes, and goes.
- **He is not a fool and not a saint.** He does not grab at her and he does not offer her anything. He tells her what
  it was — the floor of a room that was hot, and he has been in buildings for forty years and that has not happened in
  one — and says he is going to put water on it and is not going to think about it again.
- **She carries his bundle two miles up the mill road because it cannot be carried in one hand. That is the one thing
  she could do for him, it took fifty minutes, and it did not make the hand better.**
- **It works once.** On the same night the ring in the shed comes up a finger's width and stops, six times in an hour
  and a half, and it is the same fire it had been that morning. **The cost is paid, not said.**

### The third floor, and Rao — ch 380–383

- **The copying floor is where the man with the brush appears in this batch, once.** Eleven years copying down, cannot
  read the fine hand, says so and has said so three times. He tells her about the floor above his: one more flight,
  about nine steps shorter, colder at the north end. He has been up those steps twice, both times with a tray, both
  times about eleven years ago, and does not know the woman's name on it. **She spends her one question a month asking
  whether anybody is on the floor above his**, and is told that there is a stair, that there is no man at the top of
  it with a list, and that she has been standing at the bottom of it for six months looking at the top of it.
- **The third floor is the largest set of open books she will stand in front of.** Forty open at once with the leaves
  standing at every angle, a table the length of nine men, two windows that have not been opened. Cold at the north
  end all year.
- **Rao.** About forty-five, four feet ten, a thumb gone off the right hand at the first joint, sixteen years on that
  floor, forty thousand columns read. **She gives the terms of the trade in eleven words and is right about all of
  them: there is no line on that floor for a woman who walks up a stair.** Ning Yan is not angry about it for even a
  minute, and that is new.
- **Rao's structural answer is ch 391 and is Batch 0003's. She does not give it here.** What she gives in this batch
  is the shape of the floor and the price of a margin correction, and the fact that **she cannot read the fine hand
  either and has asked twice in sixteen years for somebody who can and been told there was nobody to send.**
- **Her name is given and refused and then given.** Rao asks for it on the eighth and does not ask twice. Ning Yan
  gives it on the twenty-eighth of a month, which is the batch's irreversible step.

### The first offer of a line entered for her — ch 384–385

- **Ch 384, the fourth floor price: four coppers for a margin correction in the ordinary hand, and she has eight.**
  Rao says it is four for everybody and always has been and she has never known a person try to move it. **Ning Yan
  could have paid it and does not take it, and the reason is not money and she works that out in about a minute:** a
  line she did not write is a line she cannot check, and the whole of the last seven months has been learning to read
  the shape of what is written about her.
- **Ch 385, the counter's offer, and the batch's first:** the man behind the counter offers to enter a line of her own
  in the seasonal book — the only book on nine days of road that shuts — because that is what the book is, and it is
  **ten coppers**, which is a day at that counter and ten days at Pei's gate. **She cannot pay it and she cannot take
  it, and she says the two numbers and does not say no to anything.**
- **The escalation is on the page and nobody on that ground knows it:** before the eleventh of a month there were
  three places inside nine days of road where a person could have paid four coppers to have a false line put into a
  book in the ordinary hand. **From the eleventh of a month there are none** — not hers, because her hand will not do
  it, and not anybody else's, because she has eight coppers and the wanting is not in the same place as the four.

### Mouth two of the structural answer — ch 379, day 915, the second

**A man who keeps a gate about a hundred yards short of the post, sixty, on the road between the counting house and
the post, with forty acres on one side of it.** Four or five sentences, in short sentences, and **never as a speech**:

“Forty acres here are worth what forty acres here are worth because of a thing I have never seen and could not get to if I
wanted it.” · “It is kept so that this ground stays worth farming.” · “Somebody else keeps the line. I have watched them
go past twice a day for forty years and I have never stopped one and asked him his name, and he would not have told me,
and he would have been right not to.” · “Nobody has come down that road in sixty years asking me what it is for. Not my
father. Not me.” · “You are going to stand at that post about four times a month now. And I am going to see you do it.
And I am not going to ask you about it.”

**The tenth part is not given and nothing proves the crews wrong.** It is a different statement from Volume 6's seven
mouths, from ch 365's carter and from the gate man's. **She does not sit on the bench under the sycamore**, because
sitting on a bench at a gate that is not his is a thing four people could agree about later, and she had not spent her
month's question and was not going to spend it on a bench.

### Nascent Soul 3 — ch 383, day 924, the eleventh of the month beginning 914

Taken at the seventh hour on the top floor of a building, standing at the edge of a table with a pencil in her left hand
and her right arm open, **in a room with the door shut and no other living thing in it**, and it came up out of the
boards and went under a table and forty open books and moved nothing.

[Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Nascent Soul, stage three, is held.]

**It is bracket 2 of Volume 7's six in sixty, and the form does not change at any of the six. Thirty-eight days after
Nascent Soul 2 at day 886.**

**Rao comes in ten minutes after the seventh hour, with an empty cup, and stops in the doorway.** She asks what it was,
once, and is told once, and says the room was empty and she came in after it had gone. She then says four things: that
she is the only person on nine days of road whose trade is a piece of paper and a pencil; that she is not going to
write it down; that she is not going to pretend she does not know what it is; and that a woman who has been at a table
for sixteen years can look at a room and not write down what she saw in it, **and that this is what the table is for.**

**Cost, named once, permanent, and never re-explained in any later chapter: her hand stops being able to lie.** A line
she puts in ink in her own hand is a thing she means. She can still say a false thing out loud. **She can no longer
enter a false line in any book on nine days of road and there is no hand she can pay to enter one for her.** It is not
a limb and it is not a hold and it does not touch her body at all. It is found out in about a quarter of an hour on
the bottom margin of a leaf with a pencil: she sets up to write a seven where the column says a four, and her hand
writes a four, five times, fast and slow and upside down.

### Merit settles once, on a listed clearing, told before spent

- **Ch 386–387, days 933–940 (the twentieth to the twenty-seventh of the month beginning 914).** **A ditch line at the
  north end of the yard at the turn, by the length, four feet, four hundred yards, entered on the works board on the
  thirteenth with the twenty-third against it in the margin**, because the low side of that ground goes at the end of a
  month and no ditch dug in wet will stand past it.
- **The man with the rule in his hand does not put her on it and says so twice, and then asks her for the first time
  on the twentieth, a day early, and says plainly that he is telling her now so that it is not a thing that happened to
  her tomorrow.** The sill line goes east on the twenty-second and takes the man on the ditch with it.
- **She is in the cut on the twenty-first and the twenty-second. At the fifth hour on the twenty-first the north face
  comes in on her from above and left, about a yard and a half of wet clay in about a second and a half, and her right
  arm is between it and the wall.** She gets out on her knees with her left hand in four seconds, and the arm goes into
  the other thing for two seconds and comes back. She sits on the bank for ten minutes and then gets back down and
  finishes the last hundred and sixty yards with her left hand alone.
- **Off on the twenty-second, a day early, checked by the man with the rule and two of his men, and rubbed off his own
  board with his own thumb.** **She is not on the board, her name is not in that yard, and it has never been.**
- **She cannot close her right hand afterwards. Four fingers and a thumb, and a closed fist was never in the eight of
  eleven holds.**
- **The share is three days and six hours, said out loud twice before it is spent, in his words, in the present tense,
  with the reason he is saying it twice.** She spends it on nothing. **The price is a day and a half of cold hands on
  the top floor on the twenty-fifth, in a room with a woman in it — and it is one afternoon, and not the beginning of
  anything.** **No Hand is spent in this batch. `Ledger` returns zero across all fifteen files. No figure of what is
  left of him is on any page. Nothing in this batch kills anybody.**

### Yuan — rationed, present tense, buying nothing and advising nothing

**Six utterances in fifteen chapters, outside the twenty-fourth of a month: ch 377 (one) · 379 (one) · 380 (one) ·
383 (one) · 387 (four, including the share told twice) · 389 (one).** Every sentence is in the present tense and none
of it buys anything, explains anything or moves an office. **He is readable without her and it costs nothing to say:
at ch 380 he observes that she did not ask the man with the brush for anything he could give her and asked him where
the room is.** The date of the morning he died is not given back, the unnamed state is not given back, **being sent
for is not opened, not foreshadowed in his mouth and does not happen to anybody else**, and there is no frame scene.

### Untouched, unraised, and still true at ch 389

The register still reads *reconciled* — `reconciled` returns **zero** and nothing in this batch reads it · the Clay
Plate worn against her ribs and **not described** (`plate` returns zero) · the relicensed licence **never opened** and
never referred to (`licence` and `license` both return zero) · MEMBER ABSENT carried and never raised (**returns
zero**) · **the year of non-forgiveness not mentioned once in fifteen chapters** · Shen Xiu unforgiven · *kiln-born*
zero · what became of the eleven not raised · the four months erased in Chapter 3 never named · the ch 150 refusal not
softened · **no office named at all, in any mouth, and nothing above the third floor entered, described or named** ·
the surface answer not re-voiced · the structural answer in one mouth of six and **the tenth part not given** · **the
claim not reopened** · Hou Sanliang paid, never met, not on the page · Ning Wu and Ning Bo not on the page · **Wei Ran
does not appear** · Feng does not appear and no second Lung and no second appointment · no harem and nothing a
romance · **she never says her mother's name** · **no new enemy and no named villain** · **not one word of the Order is
on the page** · `Ledger` returns zero · **nothing in this batch kills anybody** · **no warrant, no demand, no
confinement, no keeping, no debt, no family paper, no ash, no ration, no hiring, no tending, no crossing, no audit, no
examination, no signature** — the pressure of this batch is what a room thinks she is worth, and nothing else.

### Live at the last page of Batch 0002, going into Batch 0003

- **Her name is in a stair book on the top floor of the only building inside nine days of road with open books in it**,
  written in the ordinary hand at the fourth hour on the twenty-eighth of the month beginning 914 by a woman of
  forty-five. **It goes up the mill road on the first at the sixth hour with a cord round the tray, comes back down on
  the ninth, and is copied out into a book on the floor below by a man with a brush who cannot read the fine hand and
  has never been able to.** **It cannot be taken out.**
- **The furnace lights only where she is with another living thing in the room, and she cannot choose the room.** She
  is at the third floor four times a month by an arrangement she asked for out loud and cannot un-ask.
- **A man about fifty-five with a burn on his hand is two miles up the mill road at a post, has said he is not going to
  think about it again, and has never been asked for anything by her.**
- **The fine hand, and a person on a floor who has asked twice in sixteen years for somebody who can read it and been
  told there was nobody to send.** Dai is unspent and is Batch 0004's.
- **A strip went east on the first of a month about two months back and did not come back, and a column nine days of
  road east has two lines at the foot of it where it had four for four months.** Neither thread has moved in this
  batch and neither is closed.
- **A false line can no longer get into any book inside nine days of road, and no person on that ground knows it.**

### Volume 7 Batch 0002 — word-count correction

**Batch 0002's recorded 27,966 words of body prose, average ~1,864, was wrong and has been corrected here and in every
other state file that carried it. The correct figures are 28,296 and ~1,886.**

- **The method is settled and it is body-prose-only**: the `#` title line and every `##` section heading excluded.
  Batch 0001's 27,114 and Batch 0003's 29,552 both reproduce exactly under it. Headings-included counting over
  Batch 0002 returns 28,973, and the old 27,966 reproduces under neither.
- **The per-file row was part of the error.** The fifteen figures this batch recorded sum to exactly 27,966, so the

### The batch in one sentence

A woman of forty-five read a column back to her and told her, once, that the strongest thing in that building is a
column; a woman who has driven clay for eleven years told her in four sentences where the money on that ground comes
from; she put a reach of her own into a stone in a bed with a man under it and the stone went four feet sideways
and did not come back; an office asked for the holder and the holder answered upward for the first time on that
ground and the answer came down her own mill road as a question about her, and she cannot refuse a reading again; and
a cup of something hot was put into her hands at the eighth hour of the twenty-sixth of a month and she could not
take it.
