### The construction the batch is built on, and how it moves

| | Entering (day 950) | At the last page (day 1,020) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The reach | happened once, by accident, on a bank past the mill; never on purpose | **usable on purpose and four feet, and it does not go back and it does not go where she tells it.** Four deliberate uses at that yard and five holes in the low side of it |
| Her name | in one stair book on the top floor, going up the mill road on the first | **in two books, both of which shut, and she cannot get it out of either.** She has asked out loud for it to be taken out and a woman of forty-five has told her the shape of why it cannot |
| The yard at the turn | nine men where four will do, and no name of hers anywhere | **nine men who know a stone moved and not one of whom can say who moved it, and a page in a yard book that has come back down the road twice asking for a holder** |
| Her hands | cold on every road, warm in one room when a woman is in it | **the same, and she can no longer be given anything**, so the cup that came out of the office end on the twenty-sixth is standing on a bench going cold |
| Her reading | a column she could not have read back | **read to her, all of it, in the ordinary voice, on the eighth of a month** |

### The midpoint — ch 390, day 951, the eighth

**Rao reads her column back to her, all of it, in the ordinary voice, in a room, and the column contains a refusal,
and it is the only refusal in the set, and nobody on that floor has ever had one to read.**

- The column as it goes on the page: her name in a hand Rao cannot read · five months of days and hours down the
  margin · a season marked at the first end and **not marked at the second end** · a place four miles east of a
  building with a stove in it, in the ordinary hand, a third of the way down · a rule underneath it with nothing at
  the end of it · and at the foot of the page, dated the twenty-sixth, in the ordinary hand of that building,
  *Ning Yan, of Lowwater. The holder is not presented. Refused at the counter. The twenty-sixth.*
- **It says no.** Said twice, in short sentences, and the second time with the room named: **the room was cold at
  the north end and it had been cold at the north end since before she came on it.**
- **Rao has read forty thousand columns and has never read one back to the person it belongs to**, and she says the
  reason out loud: nobody on nine days of road has ever come up nine steps and asked her to.
- **The strongest thing in this building is a column.** Said **once**, in Rao's mouth, on the page, and followed in
  the same breath by *I am not going to say it a second time.* **It is not repeated anywhere in ch 390–404 and
  Volume 8 inherits a book in which it has been said exactly once.**
- **The reversal, in her own words on the mill road, and it costs nothing and undoes nothing:** she has been standing
  next to it for two months of months and working at the wrong end of it; every room that has priced her has priced
  her right, and every price has been for the hands and the arm and four feet of a bar.
- **The chapter's own sentence about a room, which is not the ch 369 refrain and does not quote it:** *Everything
  that has ever been done to me on nine days of road came out of a book in a room, and I have never once been in the
  room while it was happening.*
- **The second line at the head of her column is a name and it is not read.** Rao says she has looked at it four
  times in sixteen years, it is why the rule has nothing at the end of it, and it goes up the mill road on the
  first. **Being sent for is not opened here and is not foreshadowed in anybody's mouth.**

### Mouth three of the structural answer — ch 391, days 952–954, in Rao

Four sentences and no more, said without looking up, in the middle of reading: **what is on the line is paid for out
of a place she has never seen and has never had a road to · a column came up off that line once in eleven years with
nothing at the end of the rule and she has never found out what that meant · the room it is paid out of is not on any
strip she has ever been sent and is not in that building · and whoever sends it has never once sent anything to
ask.**

**What is new in it:** a column is a bill somebody else is paying, the season at the head of it is their season, and
an office on this ground has been sitting on an unanswered column for eleven years **and has not given up on it.**
**The tenth part is not given and nothing proves the crews wrong.**

### The reach, used on purpose — ch 393–397

- **Ch 393, days 958–959.** A listed sill course at the south end of the yard at the turn: **by the weight,
  thirty-one dressed stones, four feet of bed, off the board with the twenty-eighth against it in the margin,
  because the low side of that ground goes at the end of a month.** Nine men where four will do. A dressed stone two
  men cannot shift. On the sixteenth, at the tenth hour, a bar goes and a man's leg is under it for about nine
  seconds and he gets it out in four.
- **Ch 394, day 960 — NASCENT SOUL 4, bracket 3 of six, in the open at the seventh hour at the south end of that
  yard with nine men on it and two of them nine feet off.**

  [Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Nascent Soul, stage four, is held.]

  **Thirty-six days after Nascent Soul 3 at day 924.** **This is the one chapter in the volume that is not a bracket
  chapter and is where she hears the last four words of it and finds they have stopped being true**, because there is
  no county within nine days of road and there are people in rooms on this ground who are above her. She thinks it.
  The layer does not change.
- **What she does, on purpose, at the eighth hour of the seventeenth, with a man's leg under the stone: she puts the
  reach there.** It goes the length of her own arm and stops. **It goes sideways and about nine inches down into the
  open ground on the low side, where the clay has been scraped off, and it is not in the bed and not on the line.**
- **Cost, named once, permanently, and never re-explained in any later chapter: a thing she reaches cannot be put
  back where it was.** The bed it came out of has a lip on it. The ground it crossed stays crossed.
- **She wanted it to go up.** She worked out on the hedge on the sixteenth, before she did it, that the asking would
  move it four feet in a line it picks, and she did it anyway, and she was sick at the office end at two hundred
  yards afterwards.
- **Ch 395, days 961–968.** Four more deliberate uses in eight days, all off ends of planks and rims of barrows and
  out of a bed. The fire comes up under a man's boot at the sixth hour on the twenty-second and she cannot turn it
  off. **The man with the rule in his hand tells her the yard book is not his and does not stay in that yard and
  there is no column on this ground for what is written at the foot of a page.**
- **Ch 397, days 975–980. She tries to put it back and it opens a second hole.** Four hundred pounds of dressed
  stone, one in each, five feet apart, and four men on the bed say *they have put another one in there* and *that is
  the third*, without turning round. **And on the seventh, at the boards, at the second hour, she works out the
  difference between the two spends: the fire moves when she asks.** The five months of merit spent on nothing came
  back on the sixth by itself; a thing her hand reaches does not.

### Merit settles once — ch 395–396, on a listed clearing, told before spent

- **The line came off the works board at the fourth hour of the twenty-eighth**, checked by the man with the rule in
  his hand and two of his men, and rubbed off with his own thumb. Twenty-nine stones instead of thirty-one and two
  hundred and ten feet of bed instead of three hundred.
- **The share is four days and two hours**, out of a line that closed at the end of a month and **not off a death**,
  said out loud twice at the second hour on the boards before it is spent, with the reason it is being said twice.
- **She spends it on nothing**, out of her foundation, and it does not come back. **The price is not a limb and not a
  hold and not a count of breath: the fire stops coming up for four days** — the twenty-ninth, the first, the second,
  and the third — and on the sixth, at the fourth hour, on the top floor of the only building inside nine days of road
  with open books in it, the back of her left hand is cold against cold wood for the **first time in nineteen
  sittings**, and a woman of forty-five says *Wood’s cold*, and turns the page.
- **No Hand is spent on it and `Ledger` returns zero across all fifteen files. No figure of what is left of him is
  stated on any page. Nothing in this batch kills anybody.**

### Mouth four of the structural answer — ch 398, days 981–985, in a woman who drives clay

A woman of fifty who has driven clay up a track from a bank two hundred yards off that yard for eleven years, for
that yard and four others, with a barrow and a plank on the handles and a forearm like a man's. She says it while
tipping a barrow, in the middle of eleven trips:

> There is a household nine miles west of me that has paid me in clay every month for eleven years. It is not in this
> county and I do not know anybody in it. I have never seen the house. I go where the clay goes and I come back. And
> it has never once been late.

**What is new in it, and it is the same fact from a different mouth: somebody nine miles west of that bank has been
paying a woman nine miles east of it for eleven years out of a house she has never seen and is not able to go to.**
**The tenth part is not given and nothing proves the crews wrong.** It is not the same four sentences as ch 365, ch
379 or ch 391 and it is never a speech.

### The third frame scene — ch 401, days 993–994, and it is the book's protagonist's act

**Away from her hearing, in the layer's own reading-space, and every sentence he speaks is in the present tense.**
It is **not** the field of ch 272 and **not** the room with the two shut doors of ch 338, and the chapter says so in
its first two lines. **It is not a description of him and it is not a re-run of either.**

- **The place:** a narrow room with a low ceiling and no furniture, one strip of paper lying flat on the floor nine
  feet off, light with no source and no shadow, and no door he can find.
- **What is on it:** two lines in a hand he has never seen, saying that a line has not been carried to a room and
  that a holder is required against it. It came down at the eighth hour of the eighteenth and is not a strip that has
  been in a coat for five months.
- **He could leave it.** That is the honest shape of it and he stands there with it for nine seconds. **Being held is
  a thing that can be answered now and he has known that for four months and has not answered anything, because there
  was nothing to answer.** He turns toward it.
- **What it costs him.** He looks for the morning and it is not there and it is not going to be. He looks for the
  unnamed state and it is not there either. **All three of his prices — his unentered state, his unnamed state and
  the date of the morning he died — stay gone and none of them is given back, and none of them is re-explained.**
- **The third thing, and it is worse than the second because an answer can be quoted:** being answerable is not
  being findable and it is not being sendable. A finding can be denied, a sending can be refused once, **an answer
  cannot be refused at all, because an answer is the only one of the three that is a thing anybody can quote.**
- **What it goes out of:** the ground she is standing on, under her feet, at the second hour on the nineteenth of a
  month, in a shed at a mill whose wheel has not turned. Not out of her hands. He cannot reach into a person and has
  not been able to since he died. **He has felt the one before, at a counter, on the first of a month, and he does not
  know whose it was and he did not ask, and tonight he takes more and she will work out a floor and cannot ask.**
- **He does not tell her. She cannot hear him and he is not going to pretend to her that he can.** He does not think
  about the account, and the year still carries and the subject is not mentioned in these fifteen chapters.
- **He does not do it twice**, because one is what he has and two would be a habit, and a habit is a thing that gets
  found.

### The break — ch 402, days 995–996

**An office asks for the holder and the holder answers, on purpose, and the holding is used to move an office rather
than a cart. All three costs are named before they are spent.**

1. **Out of her foundation, told before spent.** She says it on the boards at the second hour on the nineteenth, out
   loud, to an empty room: *A holder's answer is paid out of the foundation of the person it is about.* She says it
   twice, because it is a thing she wants to hear twice. **And then she says that he will not do it, and that she
   knows that the way she knows her own hands.**
2. **The third frame scene away from her hearing, at ch 401.**
3. **She can never again refuse a reading**, and it lands in her own words in the plain part: not because there is a
   rule against it and not because anybody has decided it, but because an office on this ground now holds an answer
   that has already been given, and a second refusal would be a refusal of an answer already given, and an office that
   holds an answer already given does not accept a second one.

**How it happens on the page.** A strip came down the mill road at the eighth hour of the eighteenth and did not come
down on a first and did not come down on a ninth. The man behind the counter takes it up nine steps because that is
where a holding is kept, and Rao reads it standing up in eleven seconds and reads it twice, and writes nine words at
the top of it. **On the twenty-second, at about the eighth hour, the same strip comes into the counting house in a
way that has not happened in eleven years and there is no cart and no post and no boy on the mill road.** Under the
nine words there are two lines in a hand that has never been in that room: *The holder has answered. The holder has
been read to and the reading has been carried up and this ground has not had that happen before. The answer is
given.* And under those two, the question: *The answer names a woman refused at this counter on the twenty-sixth of a
month six months back. Is that woman at the gate three miles east of the mill.*

**She says *Yes*.** In the ordinary voice, in daylight, on a step, at four feet. He writes eleven words in the
seasonal book, which shuts, in the ordinary hand: *Ning Yan, of Lowwater. At the gate three miles east of the mill.
The twenty-second.*

**What breaks is the assumption that a line on this ground can only be read downward.** He says it on the step, in the
ordinary voice, and every part of what he says is correct.

**What is not undone.** **Nothing takes the refusal at ch 358 out of its book.** The man behind the counter says on
the step that the refusal from the twenty-sixth is still in that book, that nothing has come off it, and that he is
not going to touch it. **What has gone is not the entry. It is the second time.**

**She was not got.** She stands four hundred yards down the made road and works that out on the boards nine times,
and a man had come out of a building in daylight and told her a true thing about a piece of paper and she had answered
it, and an office on nine days of road had just read a line upward for the first time in eleven hundred years of
practice, and the price had come out of a shed three miles east and arrived at a gate without anybody carrying it.

**The yard page goes up the same road on the same cart.** The man with the rule in his hand wrote his margin note on
the twenty-first, in his own hand, exactly as he said he would on the thirteenth: **two holders are now required
against the seventeenth of a month, and there is one holder on this ground.** Three men at that yard have read it.

### Nascent Soul 5 — ch 404, day 999, bracket 4 of six

Taken at the seventh hour on the north end of the yard at the turn, in the open, in front of nine men, and it came up
under a forty-foot clay stack and through the strip of plank a man had laid down over the place where his boot had
been on the twenty-second of a month.

[Ning Yan, past the line, servant-born, under no ceiling any county holds. Nascent Soul, stage five, is held.]

**Thirty-nine days after Nascent Soul 4 at day 960.**

**Cost, named once: she cannot be given anything.** A gift that a person puts into her hand enters the column, and
the column can be read, and a thing that has been given is a thing an office can read her having been given. **She
can still be owed. She can still refuse to pay.** It is not a limb and not a hold and it does not touch her body.

**What it costs on the page: a cup of something hot.** The woman in the office end has put one into her hands
thirteen times in seven weeks and has never once asked her name and has never put a word about her in a book
anywhere on nine days of road. On the thirteenth, at the eighth hour of the twenty-sixth, **her hand goes toward the
cup and stops four inches short of it.** The woman says *Take it*, in the ordinary voice, and then *All right*, and
goes back in. She stands there a minute and a quarter and puts it on the edge of the bench with the office end
against it, which is where things go at that yard, **and the cup is hot for four minutes and then it is not hot.**

She had wanted it every one of the thirteen times and had not once thought about where it went.

### Yuan — rationed, present tense, buying nothing, advising nothing

**Five passages in fifteen chapters: ch 393 (three sentences) · ch 396 (three) · ch 397 (three, on the boards) · and
the third frame scene at ch 401, which is his act.** Every utterance is an unmarked paragraph standing alone in the
present tense. **He buys nothing, explains nothing, advises nothing and advances nothing.** **He appears in his own
frame nowhere else in ch 390–404, and ch 401 is the third frame scene in six volumes and is Batch 0003's.**

**Hand is spent once in this volume, across ch 401–402:** he lets a send reach him and answers it on the twenty-first
at day 994 in the third frame scene, the answer is carried up the holding and an office moves, and it arrives on her
own road on the twenty-second at day 995, where the break lands at ch 402. `Ledger` returns **zero** across all
fifteen files. **No figure of what is left of him is stated on any page.** **Being answerable is new as of ch 401 and
worse than being sendable, because an answer can be quoted.**

### Untouched, unraised, and still true at ch 404

The register still reads *reconciled* — **`reconciled` returns zero and nothing in this batch reads it** · the Clay
Plate worn against her ribs and **not described** (`plate` returns zero) · the relicensed licence **never opened** and
never referred to (`licence` and `license` both return zero) · MEMBER ABSENT carried and never raised (**returns
zero**) · **the year of non-forgiveness not mentioned once in fifteen chapters and still carrying** · Shen Xiu
unforgiven · *kiln-born* zero · what became of the eleven not raised · the four months erased in Chapter 3 never
named · the ch 150 refusal not softened · **no office named at all, in any mouth, and nothing above the third floor
entered, described or named** · the surface answer not re-voiced · the structural answer in **two mouths of six**, at
ch 391 and ch 398, and **the tenth part not given** · the claim not reopened · Hou Sanliang paid, never met, not on
the page · Ning Wu and Ning Bo not on the page · **Wei Ran does not appear** · Feng does not appear and no second Lung
and no second appointment · no harem and nothing a romance · **she never says her mother's name** · **the Hunter is
not on the page in this batch** and his request is still pending and the line's own people still do not know she
exists · **the fine hand and the man whose job it is are not on the page and the fine hand is not read** · **Dai and
Geng are unspent and are Batch 0004's** · **no new enemy and no named villain** · **not one word of the Order is on
the page** · `Ledger` returns zero · **nothing in this batch kills anybody** · **no warrant, no demand, no
confinement, no keeping, no debt, no family paper, no ash, no ration, no hiring, no tending, no crossing, no audit,
no examination, no signature** — the pressure of this batch is the reach used on purpose and the direction of a
sending, and nothing else.

### Live at the last page of Batch 0003, going into Batch 0004

- **She can never again refuse a reading.** The second line is in the seasonal book with the twenty-second against it
  and an office on this ground now holds an answer she gave in the ordinary voice.
- **The yard page is on its way up the mill road and comes down on the ninth** with *two holders are now required
  against the seventeenth of a month and there is one holder on this ground* in the margin, in a yard-book hand, with
  a third man who has read it.
- **Her name is in two books inside nine days of road and she has asked out loud for it to be taken out and a woman of
  forty-five has told her the shape of why it cannot.**
- **Four dressed stones in the low side of that yard under clay and dressed stone, one of which she put there
  deliberately and one of which she put there trying to undo the first.**
- **A reach that goes four feet, does not go back, and does not go where she tells it.**
- **A cup she cannot take, and a woman in an office end who will keep bringing one.**
- The strip east that did not come back · the fine hand and the man whose job it is · *kiln-born* in two heads ·
  what became of the eleven · the register still *reconciled* · the plate still worn · the year still carrying.

## Volume 7 Batch 0003 (ch 390–404) — REVIEW REPAIR

**The review of volume-07-batch-0003 returned four must-fix findings and five craft findings. All four must-fix and
four of the five craft findings are repaired. One craft finding is ruled on and left standing, with the ruling
recorded. No planned plot moved, no day-table position moved, no bracket moved, no stage moved, no title changed and
no chapter outside ch 390–404 was opened. Nine chapter passages were rewritten and every one of them was re-measured
afterwards.**

### The four must-fix findings

1. **Ch 396's arithmetic contradicted itself, in the one paragraph that performed its own check.** The paragraph set
   the sum out loud — *Fifteen at the seventh of that month. Twenty-three days at the gate at a copper a day, which is
   twenty-three in. Four trips west at eight, which is thirty-two out. Fifteen and twenty-three and thirty-two is
   six* — **and the sentence around it said she had *five* in the fold and had *got five both times*.** The text
   asserted verification and failed it. **The balance is now six throughout, and the third sentence carries the
   re-check in different words so that the verification is stated once and lands.** The same paragraph also said
   *the seventh of that month* for the fifteen, **and ch 390 carries day 951, which is the eighth** — ch 396 was
   contradicting its own batch. **Now *the eighth*.** Two faults, one paragraph, both in the fixed price table the
   prompt tells the next batch to carry exactly.
2. **The Batch 0004 prompt's money table did not match the page it claimed to quote.** It read *fifteen at the
   seventh, eleven at the second hour of the twenty-eighth, six at the end of the thirtieth, seven at the twenty-sixth
   of the next month, and four at day 1,020, with ten trips west at eight in seventy days.* Verified against the page:
   the fifteen are at the **eighth**; **eleven coppers is on no page in any of the fifteen chapters**; the balance at
   the thirtieth was **five** in the prose and is now six; the twenty-sixth carries **four**, not seven; and ch 396
   says **four** trips west, not ten. **Batch 0004 was told this was the fixed checkable table it must carry forward,
   and one figure in it did not exist.** It now carries the nine page-verified anchors, states that the steps between
   them are not carried on the page as arithmetic and that no derivation may be invented to bridge them, separates
   trips west from purse arithmetic with both spans named, and **records the struck chain so that it is not copied
   again.**
3. **Ch 404's last line spent ch 412.** It closed *the twenty-first day of the next month was in nine days and the
   board was full and none of it could pay her* — and Batch 0004's pinned beat is that **the board fills when the
   season turns and not one line on it can be entered against her, and the pay question closes at ch 412**, walked
   and not summarised, **eight chapters later.** **The line is cut.** The chapter now closes on the cost it actually
   owns: the cup on the bench, seven weeks of them behind it, another one coming inside three days, and her hand
   going to do the thing it did at the quarter past the sixth hour on the twenty-sixth. **Until the eighth hour of the
   twenty-sixth the not being able to take anything was a thing about four fingers and an arm that does not close.
   This is not about her hands at all.** That is a question opened and not a question answered, and it leaves ch 412
   entirely Batch 0004's.
4. **Ch 404's closing chronology was broken.** The sections ran *The Twenty-Seventh To The Thirtieth* → *The First Of
   The Next Month* → *The Last Of It*, and the last of them opened *She lay on the boards at the second hour of the
   twenty-eighth* — **three days before the section it follows**, and unreadable as the twenty-eighth of the new
   month, which is past the batch. **The heading is now *The Seventeenth* and the section opens at the second hour of
   the seventeenth, which is day 1,020**, so the tail runs 999 → 1,004 → 1,020 in order and **the chapter now says
   on the page what the prompt and this file have been claiming for it**, which is that the last page of Batch 0003 is
   day 1,020. The old closing line's *the twenty-one day of the next month was in nine days* could not hold from a
   twenty-eighth by any reading and is gone with the rest of the line.

### The craft findings

5. **Card instructions had leaked onto the page as section headings.** *The Cost, Named Once* at ch 404 reproduced its
   card's note, and *What It Costs Him To Be In The Place* at ch 401 did the same. **Now *The Cup And The Column* and
   *The Two Things He Goes Looking For*** — what the sections do, in the world's terms.
6. **Ch 401's closing line recycled a spent refrain.** *a habit is a thing that gets found* is ch 335's *A line is a
   thing that gets found* with the noun swapped, which is the same shape rather than a different sentence, and the
   budget had claimed 401 quoted neither. **Now reads** *the first thing that anybody on this ground does with a
   habit is put a line in front of it* — his trade, on his ground, and a sentence the volume has not used.
7. **Ch 393 ended on a flat exit line.** Its best line — *You have never once put anything anywhere on purpose. That is
   the whole of what is different tomorrow and you already know it* — sat two paragraphs from the end behind *Go and
   sleep, he said.* **The tag beat now stands where he stops buying anything and immediately before the speech, and
   the chapter ends on the line itself.** No beat, no line of his dialogue and no pinned item moved.
8. **Ch 401 narrated her judgement inside his frame.** *it was the truest thing anybody had ever said to her about
   the country she was standing in.* **RECORDED AND LEFT STANDING, on a ruling.** It is his sentence, said by him,
   about his own utterance of it at ch 272 and again at ch 338 in his own present-tense frame; the judgement is
   explicitly attributed to him in the same sentence, and **nothing about what she thought is asserted.** Recasting it
   would have broken a reprise that the Volume 6 close and the Volume 7 planning both rely on, and the review itself
   recorded this one as permissible.
9. **A *whole of* tic** at 40 instances in the batch and 131 in Volume 7. **RECORDED, NOT REPAIRED, and here is the
   count by method:** body prose, case-insensitive, whole word. **Only ch 397 uses it as a chapter ending, so it is
   not an endings breach, and it is a manuscript-level signature that belongs to a volume-scale pass and not to a
   fifteen-chapter repair.** It is named here so that Batch 0004 measures it and writes the true number down.

## Established facts, Volume 7 Batch 0004 (ch 405–420) — WHAT IT COSTS TO BE RIGHT

**Recorded by the Volume 7 Batch 0004 drafting phase, which is the last batch of Volume 7. It wrote
`chapters/volume-07/chapter-0405.md` … `chapter-0420.md` and `workspace/volume-07-close/PROMPT.md` and wrote
no chapter beyond ch 420, no second prompt and no `.done` marker, which `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` gives to the controller
alone.**

**A NOTE ON THE PROVENANCE OF THESE CHAPTERS, because it is on the record and the next phase must know it.**
An earlier run of this batch produced a partial draft of all sixteen chapters and was deferred; the controller
committed it under `novel: defer volume-07-batch-0004` and then marked `workspace/volume-07-batch-0004/.wip-conflict`.
**That work was kept and repaired rather than thrown away.** The prose was sound and the beat distribution was
sound; the day-boundary arithmetic, the purse arithmetic and five continuity errors were not. **Every figure in this
block is measured against the chapters as they now stand, not carried over from the deferred draft, and the list of
what was repaired is at the foot of this block under *Volume 7 Batch 0004 — what the drafting phase repaired*.**

### The money table — every figure below is on a page, and every one comes out of the fixed prices

**The fixed prices this ground runs on, and they did not move in this batch:** a reach at the gate is five coppers
and a fifth of a reach is one copper a day · a loaf is two coppers, four loaves is eight, and a trip to the store is
bread for five days at eight · a third of a sack of clay is three coppers · a margin correction on the third floor
is **four coppers, and it is four for everybody — still unspent, still in the fold, declined twice before this
batch and not declined again** · a line entered at the counter in the seasonal book is **ten coppers** · the yard at
the turn is nothing in money, a cup of something hot three days in seven, and no name of hers anywhere in it · every
line on the board at the works is measured by the weight or by the length and **none of them by the voice** ·
**Wei Ran's rate at the bank is four coppers a day for a man with a bar** and she says so before she is asked.

**The anchors that are on the page, in order:** four coppers carried in at ch 404 and repeated at the top of ch 405
· **five** on the 18th · **six on the 19th, seven on the 20th, eight on the 21st**, worked out standing at the rail
· **eight** taken west on the 21st and **nothing in the fold with bread for five days** · **one** on the 24th after
two days at a bank where she gave up the gate · **four** on the 27th at the yard at a turn · **five** on the 28th ·
**seven** at the counter on the 3rd of the next month, said out loud and not enough · **seven** at Pei's gate on the
4th, the day the furnace came up out of the ground between her boots · **six** on the 11th after a store trip on
the 10th · **eleven** taken west on the 16th · **twenty-nine** in the fold on the 20th of the month beginning 1,064,
spent down to **twenty-one** at the second hour of the 21st, and the margin correction is four and she did not go
up the hill.

**Trips west are counted separately and are not purse arithmetic: five are narrated in this batch** — the 21st of
the month beginning 1,004, the 10th and the 16th of the month beginning 1,034, and the 20th of the month beginning
1,064 — and the storekeeper has not asked her anything on any of them and has not given her a different price on any
of them. **No derivation is carried on the page to bridge the anchors.** She earns a copper at the gate, spends
eight on four loaves, and the next figure is stated at the next anchor. **The first draft of this batch carried
several intermediate purse figures that could not both be true; the drafting phase cut the bridge figures rather than
inventing a derivation, and what stands is the table above.**

### What this batch established, and what it closed

- **The board fills and cannot pay her.** Twenty-nine lines on the 18th, thirty-one on the 24th, thirty-four on the
  fourth, forty-one on the seventh, forty-four on the fourteenth, forty-nine on the third of the next month. **Every
  line is measured by the weight or by the length and she can take none of them, and there is no line on that rail
  that a person with four fingers and an open arm goes on.** The bare board was a season.
- **Nascent Soul 6 at ch 412 and the pay question closes for good.** A column carrying a person past nine has no
  last day on it and no figure against it. The man behind the counter is right about all three things a line needs
  and cannot open one without a figure, and he says so at the eighth hour rather than let her go away and come back in
  the spring. **She is owed four coppers for a line of her own and it was never the price.** **She would rather be
  entered than be somebody who may know something, and she was right.**
- **Nascent Soul 7 at ch 420 and the reach answers a question.** It went four feet and stopped. **The cost lands
  last, in one plain sentence:** *the reach will not go out of a room she has not been asked into, and she has not
  been asked into this one, and she has been in it for two hours.*
- **A thing of hers is in a book that shuts, twice, for nothing.** Four words in chalk in a yard-book hand at ch 408,
  going up the mill road on the ninth and copied down by a man with a brush who is right about every part of it, and
  a slip with a date and a place on it that Rao wrote in a hand that is not hers at ch 410 and did not charge for.
  **Neither is a line, neither is a column, neither is a person, and neither pays her.**
- **Four months of the counter and the third floor without a figure.** A line is ten coppers. A margin is four. A
  column is read out loud four times a month for nothing. **No office on nine days of road will enter her hours as
  wages, and the pay question does not turn again in the volume.**
- **The strip that went east did not come back** and is carried as a thread into Volume 8. The tray goes up the mill
  road on the first at the sixth hour and comes down on the ninth, with a cord round it, and there is a name in it,
  and four sheets go up inside it already written in a hand the man with the brush cannot read and does not want to.

### Volume 7 running ledger — at end of ch 420 (VOLUME 7 COMPLETE ON THE PAGE)

**Day 1,090, the twenty-seventh of the month beginning on day 1,064. She is at a shed with boards on the floor
four miles east of a mill whose wheel has not turned since before she came, in no employment, at Nascent Soul 7,
with twenty-five coppers in the fold in the inside of the door frame and bread for five days.**

- **Stage.** Nascent Soul 7. Six stages taken across about eight months: 886 · 924 · 960 · 999 · 1,037 · 1,076.
  Intervals 41, 38, 36, 39, 38 and 39 days. **Every day re-derived under `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1`.**
- **Bracket budget: six in sixty, all six spent, one at each stage** — ch 371, 383, 394, 404, 412, 420 — in the far-
  side house form, character for character, and **nowhere else in the volume**.
- **The six costs, each named once, each a different thing, and none of them re-derived anywhere in the volume:**
  the furnace will not light itself and she cannot light it alone (ch 371) · her hand stops being able to lie
  (ch 383) · a thing she reaches cannot be put back where it was (ch 394) · she cannot be given anything (ch 404) ·
  **she cannot be paid (ch 412)** · the reach will not go out of a room she has not been asked into (ch 420).
  **Three of the six are doors closing rather than things taken, and two of those three do not touch her body.**
  **None of them is a limb, a hold, a count of breath or a missing finger.**
- **Yuan.** Hand, spent once at ch 402 and refused once out loud at ch 419. Readable without her, findable, held,
  sendable, **and answerable, which is a third thing and worse than the second because an answer can be quoted.**
  His unentered state, his unnamed state and the date of the morning he died are all still gone. **The Ledger was
  not ordered, held, priced, refused or thought about in this batch, and no figure of what is left of him is stated
  on any page.** The channel is unspent in this batch and was restored once in Batch 0001 and once in Batch 0003.
- **The break.** The direction of a sending turned around at ch 402 — Batch 0003's, not this batch's. **She can never
  again refuse a reading, and that is what made ch 417 possible and what made it expensive.**
- **The refusal.** At ch 417, and it is the volume's own and the second irreversible decision in it. **She declines
  to know what the fine hand says about her, forever, on purpose, in a room, in daylight, to the only person on nine
  days of road who could have read it, with no force and nothing owed and a condition that was the ordinary
  condition. Nothing in that building failed. Nothing was written. No book will not shut.** **The refusal at ch 358
  is not this one and nothing in this batch softens it, rehearses it, queries it or mentions it in passing.**
- **Three different acts in three consecutive volumes, and they are three different shapes:** a circuit broken
  (ch 344), an entry made (ch 358), and an answer withheld (ch 417).

### Volume 7 Batch 0004 — what the drafting phase repaired

**The deferred draft was kept and repaired. These are the concrete findings, not a summary.**

1. **ch 405** — *the heavy end has been closed to her since a month two months back* was ungrammatical and had no date behind it. Now *since before she came past the last working point*.
2. **ch 406** — the purse contradicted ch 405 three times inside one chapter (five on the 19th where ch 405 had projected six; **twelve at the end of the twentieth** where the gate arithmetic gives seven; and a section heading *What She Did About The Twelve*). All three reconciled to six, seven, and *What She Did About The Seven*.
3. **ch 408** — a speech was closed twice at one point and left another open, so the file's opening and closing quotation marks balanced by accident. The doubled mark is out and each of the two speeches closes on its own.
4. **ch 408 and ch 409** — both narrated the first-of-month cart going up the mill road in near-identical words across the chapter boundary. **ch 408 now ends on the thirtieth** and ch 409 owns the first.
5. **ch 409** — *it has been eleven hundred years without a seal*. `seal` is on the banned list. Now *it has gone up that road in the open*.
6. **ch 410** — the opening listed **Rao and a woman of forty-five as two people at one table**, and Rao is the woman of forty-five. Now one person.
7. **ch 411** — she says **eight** coppers at the counter on the 3rd and ch 412 puts her at eight on the 4th after digging at the gate. An off-by-one. **She has seven on the 3rd in three places**, and the plain part reads *she had seven coppers of the ten*, which is worse and truer.
8. **ch 412 and ch 413** — the drain line is against the twenty-fourth of the month beginning 1,004 and both chapters said it was four days past on the fourth and fifth of the next month. **Ten days and eleven days.**
9. **ch 414 and ch 417** — the same fifty-word paragraph, *It came back on the twenty-third of a month at the fourth hour … and a woman of forty-five turned round and said, without looking up: Wood is cold*, stood in both chapters, **and ch 417, which is dated the nineteenth, was narrating the twenty-third.** The ch 417 copy is out and the chapter now ends on the twentieth.
10. **ch 415** — *seven coppers* on the 11th and *twelve coppers* on the 16th, with *she had not eaten since the sixth* in the same breath as bread bought on the 10th. Now **six** and **eleven**, and the hunger line is out.
11. **ch 417** — carried **a restatement of a spent principle**, *a thing she reaches cannot be put back where it was*, which was named at ch 394 and must never be re-derived. **It is out.** The cost is now paid instead of stated: a lump of clay goes in at the low end of the ring and does not come up at all.
12. **ch 417** — the word count on her own refusal was *four words* in ch 418 and *eleven words* in ch 417. **Six words**, everywhere, and the sentence is six.
13. **ch 419** — dated the **twelfth of the month beginning 1,064** in its opening and referring to her first visit to the lifting floor as **the twelfth of a month beginning 1,004**. Now two months back.
14. **ch 419** — the counter man's precedent said of an earlier refusal that *the no is still in that book*, **which weakens ch 417 by implying a refusal can be written down**. Now: *a reading about a person who is not standing in the room is the one thing my trade does not allow, and both of those books shut with nothing written in them.*
15. **ch 419 and ch 420** — the draft had **the bell going in ch 419 on the twelfth and again in ch 420 on the thirteenth**, and ch 419's *two hours* was also a two-day span. **ch 419 is now the twelfth only and ends with the bar lifted and nothing happening; ch 420 opens live on the thirteenth with the bar going out of his hands.** NS 7 stays on its pinned day.
16. **ch 420** — the two-column beat named **the seventeenth and the twenty-eighth** and also claimed the strip went up the mill road on that day, when the tray goes up on the first. Now **the eighteenth and the twenty-ninth**, which is the day the board at the works filled and is **eleven days**; and the bell's own line is **the second against the thirteenth, also eleven days**, so the chapter now carries the outline's beat exactly.
17. **ch 420** — *the eleven men in that room* three times, against nine men and eleven stones. Now nine men.
18. **ch 420** — the justification for asking the smallest person in the room to read the sheet did not hold. Now: a floor sheet is kept in the hand of the man who makes the floor, eleven men have worked under Geng and not one has ever had to read one back, and the man who taught him that hand was a boy of eleven four days east of there.
19. **ch 420** — *eight coppers* on the twentieth of the month beginning 1,064, which no sequence of gate days and store trips produces. **Twenty-nine**, spent down to twenty-one, with the four-copper margin correction named beside them and not bought, which is the volume's last money beat.
20. **Titles, hedges, intensifiers and lengths** — ch 418 retitled for the `May` false positive · the *nobody* family from 18 to 2 · `about…` from 157 to 144 · ch 416 and ch 409 raised above the 1,800 guide and ch 420 brought back under 2,700 · the three paragraphs over 90 words broken.

### Volume 7 Batch 0004 — REVIEW REPAIR

**A second pass, run against `logs/volume-07-batch-0004.review.log` after the drafting pass was saved. The findings
were arithmetic and bookkeeping rather than prose, and every one of them is listed here. The twenty drafting-phase
repairs above stand and nothing in them was undone.**

**The draft was kept and repaired, not rewritten, and no beat, day-table position, title, turn, midpoint, climax,
irreversible decision, bracket or stage moved. Nothing outside `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0405.md` …
`chapter-0420.md` was opened in the prose.**

21. **ch 420 — the volume's last page contradicted itself on the purse.** *Twenty-nine coppers* went west on the twentieth at the fifth hour and the line said she came back **at the eleventh with four loaves and nothing in the fold**, and then said she had **twenty-five** in the fold at the second hour of the twenty-first. 29 − 8 = 21. The twenty-first is now **twenty-one**. The anchor at the twentieth is untouched; the walk day has no gate hours in it and no derivation was invented to close the gap.
22. **ch 411 → ch 412 — a copper she did not earn.** Ch 411 carried **seven** three times, including on the walk up the mill road at the eighth hour of the third, and its last line said *it is ten coppers and I have got eight*. The second hour of the fourth is 2am and the run is worked seventh to ninth, so there was no gate hour between the two figures. **Seven is now the figure in both chapters**, and ch 412's two consequences of it were rewritten to match — *she would have found the other three inside a week* in place of *eight more inside a month*, twice.
23. **A false length claim was carried into the handoff as fact.** `state/book-metadata.md` and this file asserted that every chapter of Batch 0004 sat inside the 1,800–2,700 guide. **Four do not** — ch 410 at 1,759, ch 413 at 1,770, ch 419 at 1,777, ch 418 at 1,783 — all above the 1,300 floor, none padded. The claim has been replaced with the measured figures in both files. **Ch 408, 414 and 417 rose inside the guide as a side effect of the three repairs below and are now 1,839 / 1,891 / 2,108.**
24. **The Batch 0002 correction had been written down and not propagated.** 27,966 / ~1,864 still stood in `state/current.md`, `state/book-metadata.md`, `state/continuity.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md` while the close prompt carried the corrected 28,296. It now reads **28,296 / ~1,886** in all of them. **Batch 0002's per-file row summed to exactly the stale 27,966, so the row was re-derived with it** and ch 385 at 1,792 is now recorded as sitting under the guide rather than inside it. Two further stale figures were found in the same paragraph and corrected at the same time: a Batch 0001 length claim (six chapters under the guide, shortest 1,389) that described Batch 0001 *before its own repair*, and the Batch 0002 `about…` numerator, which had been measured on text Batch 0002 has since changed (175 → **154**).
25. **ch 417 restated a figure twenty lines after stating it.** *A hand that comes about nine times a year* in the opening, and then *it comes down about nine times a year* in her four-things-in-nine-seconds calculation. The second now reads *she had the rate of it inside nine seconds: eight of the nine that come down that road in a year are lists of places and dates*, which carries the same information in a different construction and ties to the sentence above it. **The rate itself is not repeated, softened or removed; ch 416 and ch 418 still state it in their own mouths.**
26. **ch 414 ended on a procedure.** *Wood is cold* and *she put her hand on the next book and turned the page* is a good image and it was the one ending in sixteen chapters that finished on a turn of a page instead of on a consequence. **It is still there** and four paragraphs now sit under it: what five days and one hour bought her, the fact that the reach came back once in nine days and came back because a woman of forty-five said a thing about wood, and the two lines that close the chapter — she will be at that gate in the morning, and she has no idea whether she will be at it in the spring. **That last one is ch 411's unanswerable third thing, restated as a cost rather than as an explanation.**
27. **ch 408 ended by looking backwards.** The closing paragraph was the five coppers she did not spend, which the chapter had already said three paragraphs earlier. It now closes on the ninth of the next month instead: the page coming back down the mill road, four words in chalk going into a book on the floor below, and **her not being in the room when it happens** — which is exactly where ch 414 opens and exactly where ch 409 opens. It is the same event seen from three ends and each one is now a different shape.
28. **ch 406 and ch 416 both carried the identical section heading `What He Said About The Hand`.** Ch 416's is now `## What It Is Not`, which is what Dai actually does in it. **The ch 406 duplicate the reviewer named — `What She Said About The Hand` beside `What He Said About The Hand` in the same chapter — does not exist on the page**; the drafting pass had already cut it to `## What She Said`, and the review's quote of it was from the deferred draft. `about…` in section headings is down from 27 to 26 and the body figure is **144 against an allowance of 150**.

**No beat, day, title, midpoint, climax, irreversible decision, bracket or stage moved.** The batch's irreversible decision is ch 417 and it is hers and it is the volume's second, and it is a refusal and not a fight, and nothing in the sixteen chapters softens it or rehearses it.

## Volume 7 close — repairs

**The Volume 7 close phase repaired nothing and says so plainly.** `workspace/volume-07-close/PROMPT.md` gives it
sixteen findings to record and forbids it from writing chapter prose, and the reason is on the record of this run:
a close that improves the volume by writing prose is a close that has become a rewrite, and the four earlier volumes
each have a record of what that costs. **This section therefore carries a count of zero for chapters opened and a
count of sixteen for findings, and it corrects the state files in place rather than the page.**

### The count

| | |
| --- | --- |
| **Chapter files opened by the close** | **0** |
| **Paragraphs, sentences or words of prose written by the close** | **0** |
| **Outlines re-planned** | **0** — `outline/volume-07.md` was read and not edited; `outline/series.md` and `outline/ending.md` were not opened |
| **Findings recorded for a repair phase** | **16** — four canon breaches, three internal contradictions, seven recorded figures that do not reproduce, two of which are recorded-and-not-repaired by judgement |
| **State files corrected in place by the close, with a note saying so** | **5** — `state/current.md`, `state/continuity.md`, `state/chapter-summaries.md`, `state/open-threads.md`, `state/book-metadata.md` |
| **Corrections to the words inside `state/current.md` made in place rather than by deletion** | **4** — the *As of* line, the *Volume* bullet, the *Chapter written* / *Next phase* bullets, and the *In-world date* bullet; plus one figure in the ch-420 position block (**twenty-five coppers** → **twenty-one**, which the Batch 0004 review already corrected on the page and which the block had not caught up with) |
| **Prompts written** | **0** — no `volume-08-*`, no second close record, no repair prompt |
| **`.done` markers written** | **0** — `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` gives that to the controller alone |

**THE CLOSE REVIEW FIX PASS ADDED NOTHING TO THE SIXTEEN AND REMOVED NOTHING.** It corrected one of them: the F1
sub-claim about `bible/world.md` was wrong and is corrected above, and the correction makes the repair smaller, not
larger. **It bound the sixteen to a phase: the repair phase runs before Volume 8 planning and Volume 8 planning does
not open until it has.** The binding table with the file and line of each finding is `state/current.md` under
`## Volume 7 close repair order — the sixteen findings, and the phase that must clear them`; the single-file version
is `workspace/volume-07-close/SUMMARY.md`.

### The sixteen, in one place

**A. Canon breaches on the page — four.**

1. **F1 — six named weekdays.** `bible/world.md`: *The world has no named weeks and no named weekdays… No narration
   in this book names a weekday, and none may.* **ch 391 ×1 (Rao's mouth), ch 395 ×3 (the man with a rule's speech
   and twice in narration), ch 399 ×1 (narration), ch 400 ×1 (narration).** Method: case-insensitive, word-bounded,
   `Monday|Tuesday|Wednesday|Thursday|Friday|Saturday|Sunday`, body prose. **Named calendar months return 0.**
   **THE REPAIR IS SIX CHAPTER LINES AND NO CANON EDIT. `bible/world.md` needs no correction and a repair phase
   that touches it is wasting the pass.** `bible/world.md:46` carries the standing rule *No narration in this book
   names a weekday, and none may*, and then a claim about the state of the grep at the time the Volume 4 close
   review made that rule: *a grep of `chapters/` for all seven weekday names now returns zero across ch 1–238*.
   **That claim is scoped to ch 1–238 and it is still literally true — re-measured, `chapters/volume-01` through
   `chapters/volume-06` returns zero for all seven names.** What is broken is the rule, in six Volume 7 chapters,
   and a rule is corrected by obeying it, not by rewriting it. The in-world substitutes are
   available on the page at every site and are set out in `state/current.md` under F1
2. **F2 — `Volume 6` in body prose at ch 417.** Method: `(?<![A-Za-z])[Vv]olume\s+\d+`, body prose. **1 hit.**
   `[Cc]hapter\s+\d+` returns **0**, so this is the only volume- or chapter-number on the page. The seven phrase
   variants the budget greps all return **0**; the bare-numeral form is outside the grep
3. **F3 — card-instruction section headings.** `## Mouth Five` at ch 409, `## Mouth Six` at ch 415. Two headings.
   Same class as ch 401 and ch 404 before the Batch 0003 review
4. **F4 — `Nai.` at ch 420, line 135.** A case-insensitive grep of `chapters/` for `nai` returns this instance and
   two substrings inside longer words (ch 363, ch 367). **It stands nowhere else in the manuscript**

**B. Internal contradictions on the page — three.**

5. **F5 — the Hunter's position, three ways.** ch 369 *two hundred yards west of the works* · ch 418 *the made road
   east of the turn* · ch 404 and ch 420 *eleven miles east of a cart track*, in the same clause in both. On the
   volume's own geography (ch 365, ch 366) eleven miles east is past the long road, where a carter is turned back.
   `Hunter` and `Bai Zhu` both return **0**, which is correct and matches Volume 6
6. **F6 — ch 378, one walk at two lengths.** `## The Mile And A Half Up The Road` and the closing paragraph against
   *two miles* three times in the body, for the same journey
7. **F7 — ch 401's cost paragraph is unresolvable between two events.** *Since he died* and *he did not know whose
   it had been* are false on the ch-378 reading; *four days and two hours* and *the twenty-eighth of a month* are
   ch 396's settlement's figure and date verbatim and do not belong to a hot floor. The reader is given no third
   thing to hold

**C. Recorded figures that do not reproduce — seven.** Six are state-file faults and one is an outline claim.

8. **F8 — Batch 0002's longest-paragraph row** claims *87 words at ch 379, no paragraph over ninety*. Measured on
   body-prose paragraphs split on the blank line: **118 at ch 379** and **105 at ch 389**. Two over the 90-word
   working limit, none over the 120-word hard limit
9. **F9 — Batch 0002's *nobody* row** claims *15 — nobody 15 · no one 0 · not one 0*. Measured **16 — nobody 15 ·
   no one 0 · not one 1 at ch 387**
10. **F10 — Batch 0002's *fortnight* row** claims *0*. Measured **1, at ch 375**
11. **F11 — Batch 0004's longest-paragraph row** claims *87 at ch 409, 410, 411, 412 and 420*. Measured **88 at ch
    414 and 88 at ch 419**, and 87 at ch 407 as well; nine chapters are at 86 or above, not five. Under 90 either way
12. **F12 — Batch 0004's doubled-word row** claims *had had does not occur*. Measured **`had had` ×2 in Batch 0004**
    (ch 411, ch 417) and **×11 across the volume** (ch 364, 372, 373, 374, 377, 381, 385, 391, 392, 411, 417). All
    grammatical English
13. **F13 — two stale per-file rows.** Batch 0004's **ch 417 at 2,099**; measured **2,108**, and 2,108 is what makes
    the batch's own recorded 30,380 come out. Batch 0001's `about…` **197**; measured **198**, the difference being
    **ch 363, recorded 6, measured 7**, with the other thirteen reproducing exactly
14. **F14 — `outline/volume-07.md`'s own structural-answer header is false at ch 409.** It claims *six new people,
    none of them one of Volume 6's seven*; ch 409's mouth is the man with the brush, who is Volume 6's ch 331
    mouth. **The page is sound — the two chapters do not open and close on the same words — and the outline's claim
    about it is not. Recorded, not repaired: the outline is not to be re-planned and nothing in it was edited**

**D. Recorded and not repaired, with the reason — the two judgement calls.**

15. **`about…`, 608 volume-wide**, method and batch split below. **A character hedge, capped and measured by every
    batch's own budget, and not banned.** Batch 0001 carries the heaviest load at 198 and the deepest chapter at 22.
    Recorded rather than swept, because the failure this file exists to prevent is the opposite one — a budget that
    reports a number nobody measured
16. **`whole of`, 160 volume-wide**, body prose, whole word, case-insensitive, maximum 7 in a chapter. Named at 131
    at the Batch 0003 review, which ruled it a manuscript-level signature belonging to a volume-scale pass and not
    to a fifteen-chapter repair. **This is a volume-scale pass and the number is written down so that one can start
    from it**

### What the close did correct

**Seven stale measurements, all in state files, all in place, none in a chapter.** F9, F10, F11, F12 and F13 are
corrected in the table immediately below rather than in the four batch budget blocks above, which are left standing
as the record of what they said. **The batch budget blocks are not rewritten**, because this file is append-only and
because a close that goes back and edits the numbers its own predecessors wrote is the same failure as a close that
edits the prose. **The close's own figures govern from this line down, and where a batch block and this block
disagree, this block is right.**

| ***As long as it takes to count …*** as a measure | **2** | ch 371 *count nine* and ch 379 *count three*. **Both are duration similes in narration or in a pause and neither is a declaration**, against an allowance of zero as a declaration. The idiom is the manuscript's own and stands at ch 351 of Volume 6 and four times in Volume 5 |

The three `announce` hits (ch 368, 371, 412) are about the announcement rule past the line, which is Volume 6's re-derivation and not the surface answer |

| **`vouch`** | **3** | substring with inflections, which is the method Volume 6's outline row uses and the only one under which its recorded six reproduces. ch 373 ×2 and ch 379 ×1. **Nothing about the second vouching having been copied upward is re-explained anywhere in the volume** |
| **`asked for`** | **12** | case-insensitive, word-bounded, body prose. Recorded for completeness; it is ordinary trade language on this ground and not a budget row |

Lowercase `order` returns 12, all of them the common noun — a sequence (ch 367, 368 ×2, 369 ×2, 372, 374), a purchase order (ch 363), and *a room is where a thing goes in an order* (ch 368) — and not one word of the Order is on Volume 7's page** |

By batch the longest is 88 (0001) · **118 (0002)** · 89 (0003) · 88 (0004). Batch 0002's recorded 87 at ch 379 is F8; Batch 0004's recorded 87 at five chapters is F11 |
