**Day 1,214 opens the month beginning 1,214 and day 1,244 opens the month beginning 1,244. Day 1,256 is the thirteenth of that month and day 1,340 minus day 1,256 is eighty-four days, which is the figure ch 464 counts into the boards and the length of his silence after the stop. All four pinned anchors from `outline/volume-08.md` §escalation sequence hold exactly: ch 455 at 1,226, ch 458 at 1,239, ch 460 at 1,247, ch 462 at 1,256.**

### The money table, every figure on a page, and it adds up

**Start at ch 450: nine coppers in the fold, no bread, water in her skin.** The rate in the building is two coppers a page, second by difficulty, third at twice the first. The floor at the end of the lane pays a yard of sill by the yard and **will not tell a stranger what a man at its crossing is paid, and says so out loud and is right.**

| Day | Fold | What moved it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1,206 | **9** | nothing bought, nothing given; her body does not want bread and has not since she was fourteen |
| 1,209 | **9 → 5** | store five miles west of the mill, **two loaves at two coppers a loaf**, the price unchanged and the storekeeper asking nothing |
| 1,211 | **5**, last bread | the second loaf, on the twenty-eighth, the last of the two bought on the twenty-sixth; nothing on the twenty-ninth and nothing after |
| 1,212–1,262 | **5, unchanged** | no spending on the page; water out of her own skin and the trough at seven miles free of charge because a copper is a quarter of a loaf |
| closes | **five coppers, no bread since the twenty-eighth of the month before last** | bar strap rebound with four inches off her own coat lining and the point of the bar |

**The eight coppers remain a credit on Wei Ran's book and not a coin; her hand will not take them. The four coppers for the fine hand were put down and swept back untaken at ch 447 and were not touched in this batch. She closes day 1,262 with five coppers and no bread: the last of her money went on day 1,209 and the last of her bread on day 1,211, fifty-one days back, because she is not hungry and does not need to be.** Nothing is paid for anything on that ground between the twenty-second of a month and the end of it, and the chapter says so.

**Corrected in this pass.** The drafting phase's table put the 9 → 5 spend on day 1,208 and said she had gone without bread since the eleventh, which re-derives from nothing in the batch — ch 450 puts the walk to the store on the twenty-sixth, which is day 1,209, and ch 451 puts the last loaf on the twenty-eighth, which is day 1,211. The wrong pair of figures reached three places outside this file: `state/current.md`, `state/open-threads.md` and `workspace/volume-08-batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, and the chapters themselves at ch 463 and ch 464. All five are corrected. **The chapter is now the authority: *the twenty-sixth of the month before last* for the last copper and *the twenty-eighth of the month before last* for the last bread, both of them named on the page.**

### The floor east of the turn, and what is on it

**A lane runs two miles east past the Order's building to a floor and a mile further to a crossing.** The floor: a long low building, a cut four feet deep and twelve across holding standing water, an oak frame four feet off the near bank on posts, a dressed sill the length of it, a chain on a post at each end, nine men on it, a low door in the far bank, a nine-foot room at the back with a board floor, a grey stove with a flat stone on it, a window with a shutter, six day books on a shelf. The shed at the floor: nine feet by six, a bench, a shelf with an unlit lamp, boards, a bar against the wall, a door that does not shut properly. The crossing: a gate on two posts with a chain, a painted board a foot square with the day's work chalked on it, a track north and south into country, a low hut with a chair on the step, and a light low in the ground.

**Nine men on a frame, six carts a day, four carts before the middle of the day, a sill measured by the length, a chain lifted four times an hour and hung not dropped.** Nothing here is an office and nothing here is named beyond what a floor calls itself.

### What this batch established, and what it closed

- **CH 450 — A FLOOR TWO MILES PAST THE BUILDING.** Xie Changyin does not come down the road for four days, and the thing that frightens her is not a term being settled but that the room goes back to its work and stops asking her anything. Bread west; eleven miles in two days; two loaves for four coppers; the shed at the mill, the roof cord, the boards, the ring. Back east, and past the building for the first time, down a lane she has watched carts come up for eleven weeks and never walked. A cut, a frame, a chain, nine men, and a man of forty on the boards with his leg wrapped and a man of thirty holding an umbrella over both of them in weather that did not need one. She is told she is not on the list and is left alone on a wall for three hours, which is the first peace in eleven weeks.
- **CH 451 — THE BOARD SHE COULD READ.** The day's sheet goes into her left hand and she cannot read a word of it, as she has never been able to read a word of any hand since she was eleven. She reads the figures: six in, eight out, two of them one cart twice, one line with no other half. At the foot, in plain words, a floor writes the shape of a day in the words the day was made of — loads by the load, a sill by the length, a chain that wants oil, a man's leg — and she can read every word of his day. **And she finds the ruled column on the left that is empty on every line of the sheet and has been under a pen for a hundred years.**
- **CH 452 — MOUTH FOUR OF SIX, IN A WOMAN OF FIFTY WHO RUNS A FLOOR.** Nine men off her frame in four years; eleven lines with a day in the left column that she did not write and nobody in her building has; the last of them a man off his feet for nineteen days. Zheng's leg, the eleventh day. A man of about forty comes to Ning Yan with the sheet after she squares it and says say the other thing, and she reads it out loud on a bank in the open and he says that is true. Zheng told you about the frame before it happened. A shed with boards and a ring with clay in it. **You do not take out a rule because you have not found the thing it is for.** She works the back books at the sixth hour for four years and finds eleven dates where the column nobody fills has one in it, ten of them earlier than the day the floor found out. She leaves every one of them exactly as she found it.
- **CH 453 — HE SURFACES A LISTING, TOLD BEFORE SPENT.** At the fifth hour on the fifth of a month, in the dark, in a shed: five days and two hours, out of the ground under her, not coming back. It is not for you. It is not for me. I am putting it where somebody's hand already is. **She asks whose hand and is refused once and does not ask twice.** He says *tonight*; it goes in the morning, and that is the only thing about it she can turn over. She stands at the crossing on the sixth, the seventh and the eighth and counts lines of chalk she cannot read, and on the eighth a man of fifty comes out of a hut, puts his thumb under a line, reads the board twice, takes a bar off the wall and goes north without asking where the work was or who wrote it or whether it will be paid.
- **CH 454 — FORTY YARDS OF SILL, AND A DITCH.** Real risk and a real deadline: forty yards of dressed stone across a made ditch, the low end loose, a man of fifty with a lever and four wedges, two days' work, the last of the light. The near end turns and takes him into the ditch on his back with his leg under the stone's shoulder. **She is forty feet off and can move it the length of its own length and nothing goes, because it does not go out of a room she has not been asked into and nobody asked.** She runs the last forty feet, gets him out in four seconds, holds the wedges for two hours in the dark while he sets it a foot at a time, and does not once make her hand remarkable. They talk for four sentences and no more. **He reads the board a sixth of a month and goes north on the eighth, finishes at last light, and walks past the low door in the far bank without turning in at it.**
- **CH 455 — FOUR DAYS APART.** The thirteenth. He comes down the lane four days after the work and tells a woman of fifty about it and she writes the thirteenth in the right-hand column. **The left-hand cell, empty on every line of that sheet for as long as she has known the room, has a date in it: the ninth.** She reads two columns against each other on her knees four inches off the paper and the two dates are four days apart, the earlier one four days before anybody in that building knew there was a sill. She cannot read a word of the hand. She works out what she has — the ninth, the thirteenth, a man who finished at last light and went home — and what she cannot do with it: eleven dates in four years, a woman who has had the book open four years and never queried it, and nothing she owns that says *this is wrong*. **If she had the fine hand the cell might carry a name and a floor and a figure, and she gave that up on purpose and is not taking it back.** She does not write on the line. She does not say the thing out loud to the woman of fifty, because she does not ask anybody to do anything, and the reason she cannot make a no take is the reason a heading did not get written over a second column nine days of road west.
- **CH 456 — WHAT A RULING IS, AND THE FIRST THING HE HAS EVER ANSWERED.** She sorts the left column across six books, thirteen lines, twelve of them ordinary work nobody on that floor could have known about on the day it was done. A column gets ruled for a thing somebody expects to have to write in it and stays empty, and once in a while a day comes that the column was not made for, and the first time it happened it would have been the man who ruled it, and he would have looked at it and said nothing. **That explains the column. It does not explain the fourth day.** And then the thing she has never once had in twenty years of reading columns: **a document with her standing on it.** She was on that cart track on the ninth and the ninth was in the book four days before the floor knew. On the twentieth she says his name once into an empty shed. **Something comes back in a quarter of a second, the first time in as long as she can remember. *I know.* She asks what he knows and he does not tell her.** He tells her one thing she did not ask for: *you have got to the column, that is further than anybody has got*, and she works out that pride in her for getting to a column is indistinguishable from a man watching something he started come out right, and she has never had to make that distinction. He says he did not wait to be asked. She says the whole of it out loud to an empty shed, which she has never done about anything, and gets nothing.
- **CH 457 — PAID ONCE FOR A THING THAT HAPPENED ONCE.** She asks a man at a gate to read a board to her, because she cannot read one, and he reads it in five pieces with his thumb over the fifth line. The sill is on it again for the twenty-first; he goes north at the seventh hour and is out four hours. The woman of fifty will not tell her the rate and says why and is right. **The sill is paid once because he has been down the lane once.** Then his mouth, in five pieces: nineteen years at the gate and eleven before that at the pits and nine on a roof and four trades and two of them bad; a wife who does not come to the gate and has not in four years and that is her choice; a yard of sill is a yard of sill, the same rate since he was forty-one, in the book at the head of the shelf in a hand that is not his; he has done that sill twice in twelve days and it has been paid for once, and nobody has come to take the other one back, and he is not going to ask for it and if it does not come he will do it twice again, because that is what the board says and he has never written on a board anything that was not there. **Nine days of road west is what two years of reading comes to.**
- **CH 458 — THE FOURTH FRAME SCENE.** Away from her hearing, in the layer's own reading-space, present tense throughout, no other character in a marked present frame, and it is not a description of him. Thirty-one days of listings and not one name on any of them. **He goes looking for one thing: does the layer honour a listing for a person who is not the Chosen One. It will not, and he worked that out on the sixth inside a quarter of an hour and has been carrying it since.** The layer has one counterparty and the counterparty is her foundation; there is no second door. **And it does not matter, because the layer has never paid anybody: what pays is a woman of fifty in a nine-foot room, out of what the floor takes in, because a man came down a lane and said he had done a thing.** The mechanism is hers and neither of them has been in the same room. He finds out that he did not put the dates in — a listing carries a piece of work, and work on this ground carries the day it happened and the day anybody found out, and the layer does not distinguish. **He finds out there is no listing in the space with him on it. He can put work where any hand will find it and he cannot point the thing at himself, and that is the finding and it took him twenty days.** He has had a fourth since the twenty-second and does not spend it, and the reason he cannot give her is that she read a column four inches off the paper.
- **CH 459 — THE CHAIN ACROSS AN EMPTY ROAD.** The thirtieth, the first, the second, the third. The chair is empty and the bar is not against the wall. A boy of fifteen lifts the chain through a cart at the eighth hour because a boy does that and does not go and find out where the man was. **She goes over the nine things she could have done and the one she cannot put down is that she could have gone four hundred yards up a cart track and looked for him, and nobody within two days of that gate has ever been asked to look for a man by a stranger who cannot read a board.** She carries bundles for two days; nobody thanks her; not one person on that floor puts a question to her in three hours. The woman of fifty says she will go out at the fourth hour on the third. She does not come back by the shut or by the ninth hour. The books closed at the ninth hour and nine men were on a frame. At the ninth hour Ning Yan walks down anyway and there is a light in the hut.
- **CH 460 — HE WAS FOUND AT THE CROSSING.** The fourth, the fourth hour. A woman of forty-five in a grey shawl in the back room. He is on the bed on his back, boots off and set square, coat on, hands crossed, leg wrapped, nothing on him and nothing out of place and the stove dead since before dark. **Nothing had been done to that man by anybody.** The woman of fifty says the whole of what she knows: the sill came on his board on the sixth, he read it that evening, he went north on the eighth, he was out two days and he was in the ditch on the second. **A man can carry a thing once. A man cannot carry the same thing twice inside a dozen days, because there is a thing that gets used up, and it is not his arm and it is not his leg, and there is no name for it on this ground.** He never came down the lane, and that is not a death, that is a man who did not come in. **Ning Yan says four words in the doorway: it was me — the voice put forty yards of sill on that board on the sixth and on the twenty-first, and he cleared both, and he was never asked, and the second one is why.** The woman of fifty answers: then you should have asked him not to go. **And she cannot. She is not on that floor and not in that lane and there is no way drawn up for a woman who has never met a man to ask him not to carry a sill. She said no out loud. Nobody believed her and nobody disbelieved her.** The book gets one line: he has not come down the lane since the thirteenth. She stands in the rain and wants to know how many there are.
- **CH 461 — TWICE FOR ONE THING, MOUTH FIVE OF SIX.** Thirteen lines in the left column across six books, twelve in four years and one from the fifteenth of this month. **The second figure against the same line went into the book on the third of a month, and she found it on the eighth: four days.** She finds him on a low wall — Zheng, thirty-four, a leg wrapped, a bar over his shoulder — and asks him one question and gets a straight answer: paid once, the sheet has two figures, and he has been in that room every ninth hour for eleven years and has never once looked at it, **because a man does not go and read the book to see what it says about his own work.** Then his mouth, in five pieces: eleven years on a frame and eight of them here; I have not asked why; a wife who does the numbers at home in a book of her own and has never once been wrong in it and does not read this one because this one is not ours to read, and she has never asked to and that is her business; a trade and a rate and he is not going to be asked about a book by a woman he met on a wall. **And she believes him, because she has done the same thing in front of a forty-year-old book and a forty-two-year-old book and a shelf of folders and has never once gone to read a book to see what it says about her.** Two people on nine days of road east of a turn, neither of them reading two books about themselves, and the cost of it is four days and a man on a bed.
- **CH 462 — SHE MAKES HIM STOP.** The thirteenth, the ninth hour, the door shut because a thing said in the open goes out into the air and she has had two years of her own answers going up a road. **Stop.** Once, in one piece: thirteen lines in a left column nobody on that floor fills; twelve of them four years old; Zheng on the fifteenth; two figures four days apart, the second entered on the third; a man at the gate who laid a sill on the ninth and the same forty yards on the twenty-first and was found on the fourth in a hut with his boots off and nothing done to him by anybody; **you told me one number, on the fifth, in this shed, and you told me before you did it, and it is the only reason I have any of this at all; you have spent three times and you told me once.** He gives her the one thing that is true and there is nothing in it to argue with: a listing is work, there is no line in the ground with him on it, he has looked, and he has never once been able to do the version where it is me. He will not promise. **Then he tells her why he told her once: because she would have asked him not to do it.** **He takes the fourth one and he puts it down**, and she feels it go, and it cost her nothing, and she did not enjoy it. She does not thank him. She does not forgive him and she is careful to keep those two apart. She does not ask what it was worth.
- **CH 463 — A MAN WITH NOTHING LEFT.** The first day after is the worst and being right about it does not help. **He says nothing, because he has nothing to spend, and a spend is the only thing he has ever had that is his.** She wants him to be angry and what she gets instead is that he gave her the truth and then stopped. She wants it not to be that he is proud of her for getting to the column. Zheng on the frame, thirty times up and down, no lean on the near rail, and she does not tell him, **because there is no version of *a voice put forty yards of sill on a board* that a man at a frame can do anything with except be frightened by, and being frightened by it would cost him his sleep and he has eight days of the leg left to sleep through.** The woman in the grey shawl puts a cloth over the chair. **The board is the gate man's office and it will be six before the month turns and whoever chalks the sixth will be standing where she is standing.** The bar's strap is rebound with four inches off her own coat lining. Five coppers, no bread. **He is not gone. He is in there and nothing is moving in it.** She says his name once into a shed and nothing comes back, and she had expected it, and expecting it was the worst part.
- **CH 464 — EIGHTY-FOUR DAYS.** She counts eighty-four marks into the edge of a board with the point out of the bar, eight rows of ten and four over, on the eighteenth, with nothing in her hands and nothing in the lane. **She does not count toward anything. Eighty-four is what you get when there is nothing on the other end of the count.** In coin, stopping costs nothing: five coppers, no bread since the twenty-eighth of the month before last, a strap she bound herself. In days it costs everything: the furnace will not light itself, every stage has cost somebody a pair of hands in a room for hours, she asked a woman of sixty-one once and got two hours of six days and it took eleven days to come, and **nobody is going to turn up at the end of a lane with two hours of a day in them for eighty-four days.** Not hungry is not a gift: a body that does not want anything is a body that had something taken out of it a long time ago by something that decided she would not be given anything. **And the sentence under all of it: for eighty-four days nobody pays for anything, and it is the first time in eight years that the reason she cannot get stronger is that she has told somebody to stop.** The woman of fifty comes to the shed, looks at the marks, tells her she has never once seen anybody count, and then tells her that a girl came down the lane twenty days before the thirteenth with a bundle of paper and stood at that table for four days and said nothing to anybody — that she knew, that her man at the frame knew, that she has been not saying anything about it for ten days as well, and **that is me being exactly the same kind of coward I was about the gate.** She goes. Ning Yan does not thank her because there is nothing to thank her for. **One more mark, well away from the rows, at the other end of the board, which is the one she is keeping for herself, and a door that does not shut properly that she does not fix.**

### The irreversible step, and what it cost

**Ch 462. She makes him stop — out loud, once, on the boards, in a shed with the door shut.** It cannot be undone: he has never once in seven years put a thing down for anybody, including her, and he has put this one down and told her so, and there is no form anywhere for taking it back. It cost her a man she will carry for the rest of the book, four hundred yards of cart track she did not walk, four steps she did not go up, and the only thing she has ever wanted to ask for. **It bought her nothing at all in coin and it cost her the ground under her feet for eighty-four days, and a stage needs a second pair of hands and nobody is paying for one.** She does not thank him, does not forgive him, does not ask what it was worth, and does not take it back. **And he does not argue it, because he does not argue, and because he has nothing to spend and there is nothing left to answer with.** The rest of the volume is the sound of a man with nothing to spend.

### Volume 8 Batch 0003 running ledger — at the last page, day 1,262

- **Ning Yan — NASCENT SOUL 8.** No stage in this batch and no bracket in this batch; the next and last bracket in the volume is at ch 470, with Nascent Soul 9. Nothing in these fifteen chapters took a limb, a hold, a count of breath or a finger.
- **Body.** Four fingers and a thumb on the right hand. Right arm open half the time. Eight of eleven holds. Short breath four counts everywhere including inside a loaded reach. Aperture cracked through and permanent. The small open place low behind her fourth rib, quiet. Not hungry, and she examines that fact and finds it is not a gift. Eighteen years old.
- **Seven costs stand, none re-derived and none broken:** the furnace will not light itself · the hand cannot write a false line · a thing she reaches cannot be put back · she cannot be given anything · she cannot be paid · the reach answers a question and only a question · she cannot call a halt — herself, once a thing she is in has started; she can still say no and cannot make the no take.
- **What she gave up and did not get back.** What the fine hand says about her. Spent at ch 417, and **nothing in ch 450–464 reopens it, softens it, queries it or mentions it in passing.** At ch 455 she names it once as the reason she cannot read the cell in the left column and says she gave it up on purpose and is not taking it back, and that is the whole of its use in this batch.
- **Hands and means.** Five coppers, no bread since the twenty-eighth of the month before last, which is day 1,211, water in her skin and the trough at seven miles. Bar strap rebound with her own coat lining. Strip folded against her ribs, refused forever on purpose, never read. Paper saying Foundation the first stage, unopened and not referred to in this batch. Clay Plate worn, undescribed.
- **Yuan.** Hand. Readable without her, findable, held, sendable, answerable and asked. **Three spends in this batch, on the sixth, the fifteenth and the twenty-first of a month beginning 1,214, out of the ground under her feet and identical to ch 402.** The first was announced before it happened; the second and the third were not, and the reason he gave for telling her once is that she would have asked him not to do it. **Told her once out of three.** A fourth was in his hand from the twenty-second and **he put it down at ch 462 and said so.** No figure of what is left of him is stated on any page. The Ledger returns 0 and the word does not appear.
- **In-world date.** Day 1,262, the nineteenth of the month beginning 1,244. Batch 0003 ran 1,206–1,262, fifty-seven days. Batch 0004 opens day 1,263, the twentieth.
- **Places she now is on nine days of road east of the turn:** the Order's gate with the chain and the cup; the building two hundred yards past it; **the lane two miles past the building; the floor with the cut and the frame; the room at the back with six day books; the shed at the end of the lane; and the crossing a mile further with its painted board, its chain and its empty chair.**
- **Still open:** the Hunter's request pending, the line's own people still not knowing she exists · everything above the offices that vouched unread and unnamed · the fine hand and the man who can read it, both spent and neither given back · *kiln-born* in two heads and on no paper · what became of the eleven · the register still *reconciled* and unread · the plate still worn and undescribed · the year carrying and mentioned zero times in fifteen chapters · MEMBER ABSENT carried, never raised · the relicensing legs in order, none invented · the claim answered, not reopened · Hou paid and never met · Ning Wu's decade a line with no mark, none of the three on the page · the strip under a stone at the mill · the carter at the cart stand · the turnstile man with his kept decision · **the young reader of twenty-six, who argued against asking her to read the tied sheet, was right, was overruled and has still not been told he was right** · the woman of twenty-two with a bad night in nine months' time · four hundred and seven names, one there by her mouth · the tied folder with its date on its string · two yeses without meaning · **a chair at a crossing that is empty and a board that will have six lines on it before the month turns** · **and a man at a gate with a wife who does not come there and a girl of nine and eleven years on a frame and two figures against one line of his work, which he has never looked at.**

### Untouched, unraised, still true at ch 464

Feng 0. Ning Wu 0. Wen 0. Shen Xiu 0. Qiu, Mei Jiu, Du Hong, the sponsors 0. **The year mentioned 0 times.** No office named, no department, floor, rank list, committee, house or title beyond recruited. The Order's budget never stated; its reason never explained. The surface answer at ch 267 not said, hinted, gestured or paraphrased; **drift 0.** The tenth part not given. **One man dies and it is the only death in the batch and the first outside a Volume 1 furnace; `kill` and `murder` return 0 and the prose says *he was not alive* and does not soften it.** No harem, nothing a romance. Mother's name never said. Register never read. Plate never described. **Licence never opened and the word never used.** Ashwood, kiln-born, MEMBER ABSENT, Anchor, Sisterhood, Scribe, bargain, Lung, appointment all 0. Nothing of the ending previewed. No stage, stage-day, relicensing leg, clearance, seal or countersignature required-or-not on any page. **Xie Changyin does not appear in this batch at all and does not learn the stop or the death; she is carried as not having come down the road for four days and then not being on the page.** Wei Ran does not appear and her card in Batch 0004 is unspent. The Hunter does not appear and both of his pages in Volume 8 are spent (428, 440).

### Volume 8 as a whole on the page, after Batch 0003, re-measured

**`chapters/volume-08/` now holds ch 421–464, forty-four chapters, 77,226 words of body prose** (the `#` title line and every `##` section heading excluded), by batch: **0001 (ch 421–434) 27,666 · 0002 (ch 435–449) 21,681 · 0003 (ch 450–464) 27,879.** **The 0001 and 0002 figures each differ by +9 and +8 words from the 27,657 and 21,673 recorded in their own blocks; the difference is not in this batch and nothing in ch 1–449 was opened, so this phase records the re-measure rather than explaining it. The 0003 figure was re-measured after the review pass.** **464 `#` headings manuscript-wide, zero collisions, longest 32 characters.** Day 1,091 to day 1,262, 172 days. **One bracketed layer line in the volume so far, at ch 434; one remains, at ch 470.**

### Construction budget — Volume 8 Batch 0003 (ch 450–464), measured by grep and not promised

Method: fifteen files in `chapters/volume-08/`, body prose (the `#` title line and every `##` section heading excluded), whole words, case-insensitive unless noted, **counted before it is claimed and the true number written here.**

| Construction | Measured across the fifteen files |

| Bracketed layer lines | **0.** Batch budget zero; the next bracket is at ch 470 and it is the last one in the volume |
| Bold · italic | **0 / 0.** Thirteen single-asterisk italics at first measure were cut to curly quotation marks or plain words before the count was taken, so the batch carries Volume 8's zero-bold, zero-italic pattern like Batches 0001 and 0002 |
| Blockquotes · code fences · tables · horizontal rules | **0 / 0 / 0 / 0** |
| All-caps standalone lines | **0** |
| Straight quotes · straight apostrophes | **0 / 0.** Every speech closed: open curly equals close curly in each of the fifteen files, verified after the final edit |
| The retired seven phrases — nobody asked · nobody was thanked · there is no form · there is no column · the record remained · nobody knew · nobody said anything | **0 as phrases.** Six uses of `nobody asked` and one of `there is no form` and one of `there is no column` stood at first measure and all eight were recast, the last two of them at ch 460 and ch 458 where they sat on load-bearing sentences |
| Named weekdays · named calendar months · named weeks | **0 / 4 / 0**, measured after the review pass. The four month hits are the modal *may* (*you may have the sheet*, *you may read every book*, *you may not have the rate*, *you may do what you want with it*). `July` at ch 453, `eleven weeks` ×4, `the week` ×2, `three weeks` ×1 and `fortnight` ×3 stood at first measure and were recast before that count was taken — **naming a weekday, a month or a week is canon in `bible/world.md` and Volume 8's allowance is zero**. **Eleven is the wrong number for that first list and the count above now reads ten: `Tuesdays` at ch 463, twice on one line, in a paragraph that carries the chapter's central argument, was listed as recast and was still on the page when the review ran.** It is recast now — *a boy of fifteen comes down the lane for carts, and a boy comes for carts, and does not stop at the chair* — and the boy, the chain and the chair are all established at ch 459 and still stand |

| The Yuan tag and one-breath lead-in family | **0** |
| `reconciled` · `plate` · `licence` · `license` · `clearance` · `countersignature` · `kiln-born` · `MEMBER ABSENT` · `Ledger` · `tribulation` · `Feng` · `Ning Wu` · `Anchor` · `Sisterhood` · `Scribe` · `bargain` · `Lung` · `appointment` | **0**, every one, on word boundaries. **The relicensed licence is never opened and is not referred to at all in this batch**, and the Foundation paper is carried without being named or opened |

| `vouch` | **0 as the word**, on a prefix match. Volume 8 is about vouching and the chapter says it in the ordinary voice at ch 464 without the word |

| `hiring` · `exam*` · `hunt*` · `audit*` · `tending` · `ration*` · `confinement` · `signature` | **0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0** on word boundaries |

Up on Batch 0002's 41 across fifteen, and the outline's own reason for that is on the page: this is the first volume in the run with a large number of people in it who do not know her, and a lane of strangers is a legitimate reason for the word to rise and not a licence for it. **The maximum is at three chapters and not two; the drafting phase named ch 459 and 460 and missed ch 452** |

| Staging vocabulary | **The lane, the boards and the hedge are the batch's geography and are not thinned below counting.** *at the end of a lane* 20 → **13**, *hands behind her head / behind her back* 15 → **2**, *a quarter of an hour* 9 → **6**, *four feet off the post* 14 → **13**. The third of those four was the review's real complaint: fifteen chapters staging one posture in the same words reads as a single vocabulary rather than as a place. *four feet off the post* is left standing because it is the crossing's own measurement — it is where a person stands to read a painted board and to chalk one, and ch 463 now says so in words |

| Chapter titles | **Fifteen, zero collisions against the 464 headings that preceded them and zero among themselves; longest 32 characters.** Every one is a name — no weather, no clock time, no date, no age, nothing restating the scene beside it. Checked by grep across every heading in `chapters/` before the batch was closed |
| Volume 8 bracket budget after this batch | **1 of 2**, at ch 434. One bracket remains in the volume and it is at ch 470 |

### Deviations from this prompt and from the outline, recorded where taken

1. **Three settlements fall in Batch 0003, not two.** `outline/volume-08.md` §Yuan's track says *twice in Batch 0003*; this batch's own prompt says ch 454 is *one of Batch 0003's two* and that ch 459–460 is *the third settling*. **All three are on the page**: the sixth (the sill, paid on the thirteenth), the fifteenth (two loads off the low end of the frame, paid twice, the second figure entered on the third of the month), and the twenty-first (the same forty yards of sill again, which is the one a man does not survive). The outline's own sentence — *not on a death, except the third one in Batch 0003, which is* — is honoured exactly: two ordinary clearings and one on a death. **The prompt wins on both counts and the outline's count of two is recorded as superseded.**
2. **The empty shed at ch 462 is the shed at the floor, not the shed at the mill.** The outline and the prompt both say *on the boards, in an empty shed*, and both are honoured, but the shed is one end of the lane and not the other: **ch 462's day anchor is pinned at 1,256 and thirteen miles of walking does not fit between the twelfth and the thirteenth, so a drafter who sent her back to the mill would have had to move the anchor and re-derive the eighty-four.** The mill shed is unspent and carries ch 478's last image. The same shed serves ch 456.
3. **At ch 456 she says his name out loud, and he answers, and that is the whole of the difference.** `outline/volume-08.md` §How she catches it has her *say his name out loud once in an empty shed*, and ch 419 has already spent that shape — *she said his name out loud, once, in an empty shed*, and got nothing back. **The drafting phase recorded this deviation as though the act had been changed to a question, and it had not.** What is on the page at ch 456 is the act the outline asked for: *She said his name out loud* · *"Yuan."* **And the chapter's own title is *She Says His Name Once*, which is true of the file.** What is new is everything around the act and none of it is in the deviation: **something comes back, in a quarter of a second, for the first time in as long as she can remember, and it is *I know*** — two words, no explanation, and she would rather he had said nothing. **The three shed beats are an escalation and the escalation is in the answer, not in the question:** ch 419, a name and nothing · ch 456, a name and one answer · ch 463, a name and nothing again, after he has spent nothing for six days · ch 462, a stop and no answer at all. **A later phase must not read this record as saying the name was not said; it was.**
4. **Xie Changyin does not appear in these fifteen chapters.** The prompt says she holds the offer open and does not learn the stop, and it was possible to carry her onto the page without violating that. **She is deliberately left off it**, because ch 449 closed on a silence held to her and the one thing she could add is a conversation, and a conversation would have been Volume 8's shape again and not this batch's. She is carried as not having come down the road for four days.
5. **The left column is the layer's, and that is stated as an accident of grammar rather than as a mechanism.** The outline asks that the second column be something the ground does. **It is, and nothing on the page says anything causes it**: a listing carries a piece of work, work on this ground carries the day it happened and the day anybody found out, and the layer does not distinguish. **Ning Yan never learns that he wrote the dates and she never learns that he wrote the work until ch 462, and the reader is never told the whole of it in one place.** The outline's *it will not, and that is the answer, and he is the one who worked out that it does not matter* is on the page at ch 458 and is not explained to her anywhere.
6. **The batch sits at 27,879 / ~1,859 and three chapters sit under the 1,800 guide** (ch 453 at 1,643, ch 459 at 1,500 and ch 461 at 1,651). Recorded rather than padded: each is one scene at scene length, every chapter is a finished scene with movement and a changed situation, none is under the 1,300 floor, and the batch is 6,197 words longer than Batch 0002's 21,681 on the same chapter count. *All three figures re-measured after the review pass.*
7. **The listing is put on a board at a gate and cleared by a man at a gate, not by a man in a room.** The prompt offers *gate, rail, board, room* and this batch takes the gate and the board together, because the man who reads a painted board at four feet every morning is the one person on that ground who does a thing off a board without asking a question, and because it puts the stranger two days' walk and a lane away from her so that *a stranger* is true on the page and not an author's word.
8. **The death is written in the ordinary register and neither `kill` nor `murder` is used once in fifteen chapters.** Recorded so the next phase does not read a zero and conclude nothing happened: **a man of fifty, nineteen years at a gate, is found on a bed at the fourth hour of the fourth day of a month with his boots off and his coat on and nothing on him, and it is established in a doorway, out loud, in daylight, by the woman who caused it, and nobody in the room can check one word of it.**


### Self-review pass — Volume 8 Batch 0003, run by the drafting phase before the phase closed

`AGENTS.md`'s quality gate asks for a reviewer. **The `novel-reviewer` subagent is not available on this tier** and returned a tier error, so the review was run in the phase against its own chapters, in writing, and the findings are recorded here rather than claimed as a pass by somebody else. Five findings were recorded and all five were fixed on the page before the phase closed. **The pass was not sufficient and two of the zeros it reported were not true when it reported them; both are named in the review block below, and this paragraph is left standing as written rather than tidied, because a self-review that overstates itself is worth more as evidence than as a corrected record.**

1. **A near-duplicated beat across two sections of ch 456 — fixed.** `## The Eleven Lines Again` and `## The Seventeenth And The Eighteenth` both opened with her getting the back books on the seventeenth and putting them on the bench and reading them, and the second opening was a restatement of the first. **This is the exact failure the Volume 8 Batch 0002 review found in that batch and the prompt named as the thing not to repeat.** The second opening was cut and the two sections now run: three attempts at the eleven lines that do not produce an answer, then the sorting, then the ruling.
2. **A repeated image in a repeated sentence — fixed.** *The rain came off the roof at the low end into a bucket that had been there for years* appeared four times across ch 460 and ch 462 and ch 464, twice inside ch 462. **The three later ones were recast** so the motif is a motif rather than a line lifted twice, and the first one, in ch 460, keeps the plainest statement of it.
3. **A second restatement of the same physical position — fixed.** *At the foot of the near post for a quarter of an hour* stood at ch 454 and again at ch 459, and ch 459 also asserted twice in one paragraph that she has never knocked on a stranger's door. Both were cut.
4. **One unplanted detail — fixed.** Ch 452's closing counted three lines of chalk and said *two more than was there on the thirtieth*, and no chapter had ever put a count on that board before it. **The count is now planted in the chapter itself** — one line on the first evening she came down the lane, three on the fifth day — and the passage no longer depends on a number the reader was never given.
5. **Two chronology seams found and closed while drafting and recorded above in the deviations**, not left standing: the sill was being reported to the floor on the ninth and had to be reported on the thirteenth, and the man had to go north on the eighth and work two days, because ch 455's pinned day is the thirteenth and ch 460 says he did the sill twice in twelve days.

**Checked by that pass, with the figure:** every chapter ends on a live beat and none on a neutral action · zero exact-duplicate paragraphs, verified by hash · no paragraph over 90 words · every speech closed in all fifteen files · zero brackets · zero bold and zero italic · zero of the retired seven phrases · zero of the banned canon tokens, including the relicensed licence, which is never opened and never named · 464 headings manuscript-wide with zero collisions and a longest of 32 characters · the day table re-derives under `((day − 134) mod 30) + 1` with no gap and no overlap and all four pinned anchors exact · **Yuan speaks in the present tense in every one of his utterances and the only marked present frame in the batch is ch 458, with no other character in it** · and every person on the page is correct, including the woman of fifty, who refuses a stranger the rate and says why, and the man at the crossing, who has been at his post nineteen years and never once asked why. **That pass also reported zero authorial scaffolding and zero named weekdays, and both were wrong; it reported the money table as adding up from nine coppers to five, which it does, and the dates in that table as sound, which they were not.** The block below is the record of the difference.

### Volume 8 Batch 0003 — external review, and the repair pass that answered it

`logs/volume-08-batch-0003.review.log`. The `novel-reviewer` subagent failed on this tier for the same reason it failed to the drafting phase, so the review was run against the chapters directly. **It returned five must-fix findings and four should-fix findings, and every one of them was real. Three of the five must-fixes were false zeros this file had published, which is the failure mode worth remembering: a state file that claims a clean count is not evidence, and a phase that verifies by its own earlier note has verified nothing.**

| # | Finding | Cleared |
| --- | --- | --- |
| M1 | **A named weekday on the page**, `ch 463` — *a boy comes down the lane on Tuesdays and Tuesdays are for carts*, twice on one line in the paragraph that carries the chapter. This file's construction budget said it had been recast. It had not, and the false claim had already propagated into `workspace/volume-08-batch-0004/PROMPT.md` | **Recast on the page.** The boy now comes down the lane for carts and does not stop at the chair. Both the row and the next-phase prompt corrected |
| M2 | **The bread and copper ledger contradicted the batch's own chapters.** *No bread since the eleventh* and *not spent a copper in seven days* re-derive from nothing: ch 450 puts the store on the twenty-sixth, ch 451 puts the last loaf on the twenty-eighth, and both had been pushed into three state files | **Corrected on the page and in four files.** See the money table above. The chapter is now the authority and names both dates in its own words |
| M3 | **Authorial scaffolding in body prose**, `ch 464` — *more than she has wanted anything in this batch*, on the chapter's closing beat, against a row that claimed 0 on a pattern containing `(in\|another) batch` | **Recast.** *More than she has wanted anything in her life* |
| M4 | **Deviation 3 was false.** It claimed ch 456 says a question and not his name; ch 456 says his name, the chapter title says so, and `state/chapter-summaries.md` says so. A later phase would have believed the record and written against the file | **The record is corrected to the page**, not the page to the record: the beat is the outline's, the new thing is the answer, and the four shed beats are set out as an escalation in the answers |
| M5 | *(carried in M1's row of the construction budget)* the `nobody` maximum was named as ch 459 and 460; ch 452 also reaches 8 | **Corrected to three chapters** |
| S1 | **A date slip in the money table** — the 9 → 5 spend on 1,208, where ch 450 says the twenty-sixth, which is 1,209 | **Corrected**, with a day added for the last loaf |
| S2 | **Repetition clusters** — *at the end of a lane* 20, *on nine days of road* 18, *I am not going to* 10, *that is the whole of* + *is the whole of what* 19, *hands behind her head/back* 15, *a quarter of an hour* 9 | **The lane and the hands cut by two thirds**; the nine-days-of-road and *whole of* counts left, being the spine of the voice. Figures in the construction budget |
| S3 | **Same-word sentence-opening runs** — six in ch 456, 458 and 460, five in ch 450, 455 and 459 | **All six cut.** Longest run in the batch is now 4 and the anaphora that carries meaning is untouched |

**What was not changed.** No chapter was restarted. No beat, date anchor, coin figure, character mouth, death, listing, spend or stop moved. The irreversible step at ch 462 stands exactly as written and **the review agreed with it, called it genuine, and confirmed that all fifteen cards land**. Fourteen of the fifteen chapters were opened; the prose is the same prose with thirty-seven sentences touched, every one of them a scaffolding word, a duplicated posture, a duplicated phrase or a wrong date. **The one thing this repair changed that is not a defect fix is that ch 463 now plants where the boy comes from instead of naming a day of the week, and ch 464's closing line is now the strongest sentence it could have been** — which is what a review is for.

## Established facts, Volume 8 Batch 0004 (ch 465–478) — REFUSING TO BE A TOOL

**Fourteen chapters, day 1,263 to day 1,340, 29,125 words of body prose, average ~2,080, after the batch review repair recorded below.** Body prose is the `#` title line excluded and every `##` section heading counted as prose. Per file: **465 2,463 · 466 1,817 · 467 2,313 · 468 1,873 · 469 1,796 · 470 2,560 · 471 2,042 · 472 2,109 · 473 2,041 · 474 2,549 · 475 1,950 · 476 1,825 · 477 1,908 · 478 1,879.** Every chapter is above the 1,300 floor and none is above the 2,700 guide; **ch 469 at 1,796 is the only chapter under the 1,800 guide in the batch.** No paragraph over 95 words; longest 95 at ch 471, five paragraphs over 90, 915 paragraphs measured. **No chapter in ch 1–464 was opened.** The figures this block carried before the repair — 29,186, average ~2,085, per file 465 2,453 · 466 1,819 · 467 2,313 · 468 1,873 · 469 1,796 · 470 2,569 · 471 2,042 · 472 2,109 · 473 2,042 · 474 2,602 · 475 1,950 · 476 1,814 · 477 1,908 · 478 1,896 — are corrected in place and were correct when taken.**

**Method note, corrected a second time in place by the Volume 8 close, which settles the convention.** The note as it stood said the script counted *every line not beginning `#`*, which returns **27,666 / 21,681 / 27,879 / 28,620** for Batches 0001–0004 and **105,846** for the volume. **The figures the batch and volume tables above actually carry — 28,334 / 21,869 / 28,341 / 29,125 and 107,669 — come from a script that excludes the `#` chapter title line only and counts `##` section headings as prose.** **The 668 / 188 / 462 that the Batch 0004 phase recorded as unexplained is exactly the `##` section-heading word count in each of Batches 0001, 0002 and 0003: 28,334 − 27,666 = 668 · 21,869 − 21,681 = 188 · 28,341 − 27,879 = 462.** Those three batches measured their own tables with headings excluded and the volume re-measure counted them. **No batch figure was ever wrong and no chapter before ch 465 was touched. Two conventions were in use at once and the note misdescribed which one ran.**

**THE CONVENTION IS NOW SETTLED, ONCE, FOR VOLUME 8. METHOD B IS THE HOUSE METHOD FOR VOLUME 8: body prose is every line not beginning `#`, so the `#` chapter title line and every `##` section heading are excluded.** Method B is adopted because Batches 0001, 0002 and 0003 each measured their own tables under it, because Volume 7's close used it, and because it is the more conservative of the two. **On method B the volume is 105,846 words of body prose over fifty-eight chapters, average ~1,825, by batch 27,666 · 21,681 · 27,879 · 28,620. Method A — the `#` title line excluded and every `##` heading counted as prose — is recorded beside every figure in this run and travels with it: 28,334 · 21,869 · 28,341 · 29,125, volume 107,669, average ~1,856. The block above is left standing as it was written on method A and was correct when taken; where a batch block and the Volume 8 close block disagree, the close block is right.** Full table at §*Volume 8 close — the volume as a whole*.
